Ibn ʿUfayr related to us; Ibn Lahīʿa related to us, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Mukhays ibn Ẓabyān, from a man of Judhām, from Mālik ibn ʿAtāhiya, who said: “I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: ‘If you encounter a ʿāshir [tax collector], kill him.’”

Ibn ʿUfayr related to us; Ibn Lahīʿa related to us, who said: “Sharḥabīl ibn Ḥasana was in charge of the customs levy, and Maslama ibn Mukhallad was in charge of the mills.”

He said: Then Sulaym ibn ʿItr al-Tujībī was appointed to the judgeship during the days of Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān. He had encountered ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb and attended his sermon at al-Jābiya, and both preaching and judging were entrusted to him.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd al-Muqriʾ related to us; Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ related to us; al-Ḥajjāj ibn Shaddād al-Ṣanʿānī related to us that Abū Ṣāliḥ Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ghifārī had informed him that Sulaym ibn ʿItr al-Tujībī used to preach to the people while standing. Ṣila ibn al-Ḥārith al-Ghifārī, who was one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ said to him: “By God, we had not abandoned the covenant of our Prophet, nor severed our bonds of kinship, until you and your companions stood up among us”.

He said: Sulaym ibn ʿItr was, as Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr related to us, one of the most devoted and diligent worshippers. He would stand in prayer at night, beginning the Qurʾān until he completed it. Then he would go to his wife and satisfy his desire with her; then he would get up and bathe, then recite until he completed the Qurʾān, then go to his wife and satisfy his desire with her. He would sometimes do that several times in one night. When he died, his wife said: “May God have mercy on you. By God, you used to please your Lord and bring joy to your family.”

Ibn Abī Maryam and Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Salām related to us, from Ḍimām ibn Ismāʿīl, from Sulaym ibn ʿItr, who said: “I left Alexandria—I think he said, ‘when I had come in from the sea’—and entered a cave, where I devoted myself to worship for seven days. Had I not feared that I would grow weak, I would have completed ten.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār informed us; Ibn Lahīʿa related to us, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, who said: Sulaym ibn ʿItr said to me, “When you meet Abū Hurayra, convey my greeting to him and tell him that I have prayed for him and for his mother.” I met him and told him. He said, “And I have prayed for him and for his mother.”

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related to us; Mūsā ibn ʿAlī related to us, from his father, who said: We set out from Egypt as pilgrims, and Sulaym ibn ʿItr said to me, “Convey my greeting to Abū Hurayra and tell him that this morning I prayed for forgiveness for him and his mother.” He said: I met him and told him this. Abū Hurayra said, “And this morning I prayed for forgiveness for him and his family.” Then Abū Hurayra said, “How did you leave Umm Khannūr?” I told him of its fertility and abundance. He said, “Indeed, it is the first of the lands to be laid waste; Armenia will follow it.” I said, “Did you hear that from the Messenger of God ﷺ?” He replied, “Or from Kaʿb, the scholar of the two Scriptures.”

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related to us; Bakr ibn Muḍar related to us, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Zajr, from al-Haytham ibn Khālid, from his cousin Sulaym ibn ʿItr, who said: We met Kurayb ibn Abraha riding, with a young servant of his walking behind him. We said, “Abū Rashdīn, why did you not let the servant ride?” He said, “How could I carry a foreigner such as this?”—or words to that effect. He said, “Then why did you not take a young servant whom you could carry behind you?” He replied, “I did not do that.” He said, “Then why did you not order the servant to go ahead of you, so that you could catch up with him?” He replied, “I did not do that.” He said, “For I heard Abū al-Dardāʾ say: ‘A servant never ceases to grow more distant from God whenever he walks behind him.’”

He said: Then Muslima ibn Mukhallad was appointed over the province, and Egypt and the Maghrib were placed under his authority. He was the first governor to have both. He appointed al-Sāʾib ibn Hishām ibn ʿAmr, one of Banū Mālik ibn Ḥisl, as chief of his police. Regarding Hishām ibn ʿAmr, Ḥassān ibn Thābit says:

Will the Umayyads truly fulfill a pledge, as Hishām fulfilled the obligation of protection?

He belonged to a people who do not betray their neighbor, the people of al-Ḥārith ibn Ḥabīb ibn Sukhām.

When Banū Ḥisl grant protection under a pledge, they fulfill it and send their neighbor on his way in safety.

He said: Hishām ibn ʿAmr was one of the men who took part in tearing up the document that Quraysh had written. He said: ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ had appointed al-Sāʾib ibn Hishām after Khārija ibn Ḥudhāfa; he had also been in charge of the police under ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Sarḥ. Abū Sarḥ’s name was ʿUwayf, as Muḥammad ibn Idrīs al-Rāzī related to us.

Then Maslama ibn Mukhallad dismissed al-Sāʾib and appointed ʿĀbis ibn Saʿīd al-Murādī over the police; then he combined the judgeship with the police for him. He is the man associated with Kūm ʿĀbis, which is in Fusṭāṭ of Egypt. Concerning it, the poet says:

I yearn for Alexandria, for I have there brothers in religion, people of keen emulation. Abū al-Ḥārith al-Māḍī and Ashhab are among them, two imams of guidance in Sunna and analogical reasoning. A church has been newly built there by the Byzantines, for a tyrant, deserving the eyes of spies [the phrase is uncertain]. Would that, by consultation, it had been made an empty, level plain like the ground of Kūm ʿĀbis.

By Abū al-Ḥārith he means al-Layth ibn Saʿd, and by Ashhab, Ashhab ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz al-Qaysī, one of the companions of Mālik ibn Anas.

ʿĀbis ibn Saʿīd remained judge until Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam entered Egypt. His entry, as Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr related to us from al-Layth ibn Saʿd, was in the year sixty-five. He said:

“Where is your judge?” So ʿĀbis ibn Saʿīd was summoned to him. He was an unlettered man who could not write. Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam said to him:

“Have you mastered the Book of God?” He said: “No.” He said: “Have you mastered the laws of inheritance?” He said: “No.” He said: “Then by what do you judge?”

He said: “I judge by what I know, and ask about what I do not know.” He said: “You are the judge”.

He said: The reason Maslama ibn Mukhallad dismissed al-Sāʾib ibn Hishām and appointed ʿĀbis ibn Saʿīd was that Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān wrote to Maslama ibn Mukhallad—who was then governor of the province—ordering him to secure the pledge of allegiance for Yazīd. The letter reached Maslama while he was in Alexandria, so he wrote to al-Sāʾib Ibn Hishām, who was then under his authority. The people gave the pledge, except for ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ. Muslimah sent him the letter again, but he did not do so. Muslimah said, “Who will deal with ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr?” ʿĀbis ibn Saʿīd said, “I will.” He then came to al-Fusṭāṭ and sent for ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, but he did not come. ʿĀbis called for fire and wood to burn down his palace. Then ʿAbd Allāh came and gave the pledge. ʿĀbis continued as judge and chief of police until he died during the days of ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān, in the year sixty-eight.

It is said that Muslimah ibn Mukhallad wrote to al-Sāʾib ibn Hishām to take the pledge of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr to Yazīd only after the death of Muʿāwiyah ibn Abī Sufyān. Ibn Bukayr said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Lahīʿah related to me, from Abū Qabīl, who said: When Muʿāwiyah died and Yazīd was appointed his successor, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr disliked giving the pledge to Yazīd, while Muslimah was in Alexandria. Muslimah sent to him Kurayb ibn Abraha and ʿĀbis ibn Saʿīd. They went in to him, accompanied by Sulaym ibn ʿItr, who was then judge and preacher. They admonished ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr concerning the pledge to Yazīd. ʿAbd Allāh said, “By God, I know more about Yazīd’s affair than you do. I was the first of the people to tell Muʿāwiyah that he would appoint him as his successor, but I wanted him to be the one to receive my pledge.” He said to Kurayb, “Do you know what you are like? You are like a great palace in a desert, where people overtaken by the heat gather and enter to find shade. Then they find it filled with people’s meeting places. Your name is known among the Arabs, Kurayb ibn Abraha, but you have nothing to offer. As for you, ʿĀbis ibn Saʿīd, you have sold your Hereafter for your worldly life. And as for you, Sulaym ibn ʿItr, you were a preacher, and you had two angels with you to support you and remind you. Then you became a judge, and now you have two devils with you, leading you astray from the truth and tempting you.”

Then ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān appointed Bashīr ibn al-Naḍr al-Muzanī as judge.

My brother Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh related to me: Wahb Allāh ibn Rāshid related to us, from Ḥaywah ibn Shurayḥ, from Jaʿfar ibn Rabīʿah, that Bashīr ibn al-Naḍr had been judge before Ibn Ḥujayrah, during the time of ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān.

He said: Then ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥujayrah al-Khawlānī was appointed. He was Ibn Ḥujayrah the Elder. He had met Abū Hurayrah and Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī, and people transmitted reports from him. He was given the judgeship, preaching, and the treasury.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī al-Samḥ related from Abū al-Layth al-ʿAlāʾ ibn ʿĀṣim, the preacher, that Ibn Ḥujayrah the Elder held the judgeship, preaching, and the treasury under ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān. He received two hundred dinars as his stipend for the judgeship, two hundred dinars for preaching, and two hundred dinars for the treasury. His grant was two hundred dinars, and his gift was two hundred dinars. Thus, he received one thousand dinars a year. A full year would not pass with him possessing enough to make the alms-tax due. He continued as judge until he died in the year eighty-three.

It is said instead that he was appointed in the year eighty-three and died in the year eighty-five. Ibn Lahīʿah related from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Mughīrah that a man asked Ibn ʿAbbās about a question, and he said, “You ask me, when Ibn Ḥujayrah is among you?”

Al-Layth ibn Saʿd related from Ibn Lahīʿah, from Mūsā ibn Wardān, that Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib said to him, “Convey my greetings to Ibn Ḥujayrah, and tell him to restrain the people of his town from usury, for I have been told that it is widespread there. I heard ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān ﵁—say from the pulpit: ‘I used to buy dates in the market of Banū Qaynuqāʿ, then bring them to Medina and unload them for the people there. I would tell them the measure they contained; they would give me whatever profit I was content with and take them on my word, without measuring them. This reached the Messenger of God ﷺ and he said: “ʿUthmān, when you buy, measure; and when you sell, measure.”’”

Then Mālik ibn Sharāḥīl al-Khawlānī was appointed judge in the year eighty-three. He was the patron of the mosque of Mālik in the Fusṭāṭ of Egypt. Al-Ḥajjāj used to send him a robe and three thousand dirhams every year. He continued as judge until he died.

After him, Yūnus ibn ʿAṭiyyah al-Ḥaḍramī was appointed judge. He was given both the police and the judgeship, and continued as judge until he died in the year eighty-six.

He said: Some of the town’s elders claimed that Aws, the son of Yūnus ibn ʿAṭiyyah’s brother, was appointed judge after his uncle Yūnus ibn ʿAṭiyyah.

Then ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muʿāwiyah ibn Ḥudayj al-Kindī was appointed. He was given both the judgeship and the police, and continued in that post until ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān died.

He said: The plague had struck al-Fusṭāṭ, as Saʿīd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Talīd and others related to us. Some of them mention what others do not. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān left al-Fusṭāṭ and settled at Ḥulwān, going into the desert, at a place there called Abū Qarqūr. It was the source of the spring that ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān had dug and channelled to his palm trees, which he had planted at Ḥulwān. Ibn Ḥudayj used to send ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz news each day of what was happening in the town, deaths and other matters.

One day he sent him a messenger, who came to him. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz asked him, “What is your name?” He answered, “Abū Ṭālib.” This weighed heavily on ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz and angered him, and he said, “I ask you your name, and you say Abū Ṭālib! What is your name?” He replied, “Mudrik.” ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz took this as a favorable omen. During that expedition he fell ill and died there. He was carried by sea toward al-Fusṭāṭ, but the wind grew so strong that he did not reach al-Fusṭāṭ before his body had changed. He was put ashore at one of the private estates on the coast of Marīs, where he was washed; his bier was brought out from there. Incense burners containing aloeswood were carried with him because his smell had changed. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz had instructed that, when he died, his bier should pass by the residence of Janāb, who had been his friend. Janāb had died before ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, so ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz’s bier was carried past his door. Janāb’s family came out, dressed in black, and the women stood at the door crying out. Then they followed the bier to the cemetery. Janāb was the owner of the two palaces of Janāb in the Fusṭāṭ of Egypt, one of which is now attributed to Ibn Yarīm.

The poet Nuṣayb had come to ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān while he was ill and asked permission to visit him. He was told, “He is overcome.” Nuṣayb said, “Ask permission for me; if he grants it, then so be it.” Nuṣayb had some standing with ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, and he was admitted. When he saw how gravely ill ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz was, he began to recite:

We visit our master, and the master of others;

If only the one who suffers were among those who visit him.

If a ransom could be accepted, I would ransom him

With the best of my inherited and newly acquired wealth.

When ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz heard his voice, he opened his eyes and ordered that Nuṣayb be given one thousand dinars. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz’s family took this as a good omen and rejoiced.

Then he died. His death, as Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr related to us from al-Layth ibn Saʿd, was on the night of Monday, twelve nights having passed in Jumādā al-Ūlā, in the year eighty-six. Al-Farazdaq said of this:

O you who wish to be a young man

Like Ibn Laylā: the paths are open to you.

Recall three virtues known of him:

Did he ever insult anyone, or was he insulted, or did he act miserly?

Were all the people, from farthest to nearest,

To beat the camels across the breadth of the earth,

Seeking the finest man on earth, they would not find

One like the man they laid in his grave.

When ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān died, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān appointed ʿUmar ibn Marwān over the people of Egypt. He served for a month less one night, then was dismissed, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik was appointed.

He was the patron of the mosque of ʿAbd Allāh in the Fusṭāṭ of Egypt, and it is attributed to him. When ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-ʿUmarī came to Egypt as judge, some of the townspeople mistakenly told him that the mosque belonged to ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. He restored it and repaired it well, but it is the mosque of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik; there is no doubt about it.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik wanted to dismiss Ibn Ḥudayj, but he was embarrassed to dismiss him without cause and could find no charge or pretext against him. So he appointed him to guard the frontier at Alexandria, and appointed ʿImrān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sharāḥīl ibn Ḥasnah to the judgeship and the police. ʿImrān remained in that post until the year eighty-nine. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik became angry with him over something that was not specified to me. He imprisoned him in a room and ordered that a garment be made for him out of sheets of paper, with his faults and failings written on it, then that he be made to wear it and displayed to the people until ʿAbd Allāh returned from his expedition.

He appointed ʿAbd al-Aʿlā ibn Khālid ibn Thābit al-Fahmī in his place. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik went to Wasīm, which belonged to a Copt. The man asked ʿAbd Allāh to come to his house and offered him one hundred thousand dinars. So ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik went to him.

Ibn ʿUfayr said: ʿAbd Allāh had in fact gone to Abū al-Namrus with one of the scribes, a man called Ibn Ḥanẓalah. His house was the one in which Abū Ṣāliḥ al-Ḥarrānī lives today. The news of his dismissal and the appointment of Qurrah ibn Sharīk al-ʿAbsī reached ʿAbd Allāh while he was there.

Ibn ʿUfayr said: When he heard the news, he got up to put on his trousers and put them on backwards. He said: Qurrah ibn Sharīk arrived with three couriers. He entered the mosque, prayed in the prayer niche, then sat cross-legged. One of the two men sat beside him, while the other stood at his head. A man from the mosque police came to ʿAbd al-Aʿlā ibn Khālid and said to him, “A man has arrived with three couriers. He came down at the mosque door, then entered the prayer niche, prayed, and sat cross-legged.” Ibn Rifāʿah came to him and greeted him without using the title “Commander.” Qurrah said to him, “Do you hold some office?” He answered, “Yes, I am in charge of the police.” Qurrah said, “Go and seal the register.” He replied, “If you are in charge of the land tax, this is not within your authority.” Qurrah said, “Go, as you have been ordered.” Ibn Rifāʿah then said, “Peace be upon you, Commander, and God’s mercy.” Qurrah asked him, “Which tribe are you from?” He said, “From Fahm.” Qurrah said:

You will find no man of Fahm but one steadfast,

Upholding the finest character, and learned in the truth.

I shall praise Fahm with praise that brings it joy,

That reaches the people of the villages and the seasonal fairs.

This is how Ibn ʿUfayr related it.

It is also said that, when Qurrah arrived, a man from the police went to Ibn Rifāʿah and said to him, “A man has arrived with three couriers. He entered the prayer niche and prayed, then sent one man to seal the register and another to seal the treasury.” Ibn Rifāʿah came to him and greeted him without using the title “Commander.” Qurrah asked him, “Do you hold some office?” He replied, “Yes, I am in charge of the police.” Qurrah said, “Stay in the post you held.” Ibn Rifāʿah greeted him again, this time using the title “Commander,” and Qurrah confirmed him in the post he had held.

Ibn Bukayr said: Qurrah had ordered that ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik not be interfered with regarding anything he took with him, and that he be kept from taking anything he had left behind. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik took all that belonged to him and set out for the horse estate. Qurrah ibn Sharīk did not interfere with him. ʿAbd Allāh had acquired a Turkish tent in al-Jazīrah and forgotten it; he sent someone to retrieve it, but Qurrah prevented him. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik set out with everything he had with him. When he reached al-Urdunn, al-Walīd sent someone to seize all of it.

Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥujayrah al-Khawlānī was appointed. He was Ibn Ḥujayrah the Younger. He was dismissed in the year ninety-three.

Some of the town’s elders claimed that, when Ibn Ḥujayrah was appointed preacher, the news reached his father, who was in Jerusalem. He said, “Praise be to God! My son has been mentioned and has reminded others.” When news reached him that his son had been appointed judge, he said, “We belong to God,” and, I think, he said, “My son is lost, and he has brought ruin.”

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: I do not know which Ibn Ḥujayrah he meant, the elder or the younger.

Then ʿIyāḍ ibn ʿUbayd Allāh al-Azdī, then al-Salāmī, was appointed. News of his appointment as judge reached him while he was the agent of Usāmah ibn Zayd al-Tanūkhī over the granary.

He continued as judge until he was dismissed in the year ninety-eight, and Ibn Ḥujayrah was restored to the judgeship. Then he was dismissed, and ʿIyāḍ ibn ʿUbayd Allāh was reinstated. He continued as judge until he was dismissed in the year one hundred.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khudhāmir was appointed, then dismissed from the judgeship in the year one hundred and two.

Then Yaḥyā ibn Maymūn al-Ḥaḍramī was appointed. ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith and Ibn Lahīʿah related reports from him. He continued as judge until he was dismissed in the year one hundred and fourteen. His tenure was not praiseworthy.

Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr related to us: I heard al-Mufaḍḍal ibn Faḍālah say, “He was a wretched judge.”

Then Yazīd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khudhāmir was appointed, then dismissed.

Then al-Khayyār ibn Khālid al-Mudlijī was appointed. He served as judge for about a year, then died. His death was in the year one hundred and fifteen. He was held in high regard and followed a fine course.

Then Tawbah ibn Namir al-Ḥaḍramī was appointed. Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr related to us: al-Mufaḍḍal ibn Faḍālah related to us, saying: When Tawbah ibn Namir was appointed judge, he summoned his wife and said to her, “How have you found my companionship toward you?” She said, “May God reward you well as a husband.” He said, “You know the trouble we have suffered from people’s affairs. You are divorced!” She cried out. He said to her, “If you speak to me about a litigant, or remind me of one.” He said: She would see that his inkpot needed water, but would not ask for it to be replenished, for fear that something might enter into his oath. Tawbah ibn Namir served as judge for as long as God willed, then asked to be relieved. He was told, “Advise us whom we should appoint.” He said, “My secretary, Khayr ibn Nuʿaym.”

Khayr ibn Nuʿaym al-Ḥaḍramī was appointed. He continued as judge until he was dismissed in the year one hundred and twenty-eight.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Salām ibn Abī Sālim al-Jayshānī was appointed. He continued as judge until the arrival of the Musawwidah; he was dismissed from the judgeship and appointed over the land tax. Khayr ibn Nuʿaym was reinstated and continued as judge until he was dismissed in the year one hundred and thirty-five.

The reason for his dismissal, as Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr related to us, was that a soldier accused a man of fornication, and the man brought a complaint against him to Khayr. Khayr established the case on the basis of a single witness and ordered the soldier imprisoned until the complainant could produce another witness. Abū ʿAwn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Yazīd sent someone, who released the soldier from prison. Khayr withdrew, sat at home, and ceased issuing judgments. Abū ʿAwn sent to him, and Khayr said, “No, not until the soldier is returned to his place.” He was not returned, and Khayr held to his resolve. They said to him, “Then advise us whom we should appoint.” He said, “My secretary, Ghawth ibn Sulaymān.”

Ghawth ibn Sulaymān al-Ḥaḍramī was appointed. He continued as judge until he went with Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAlī on the summer campaign in the year one hundred and forty-four.

Then Abū Khuzaymah Ibrāhīm ibn Yazīd al-Thātī—a branch of Ḥimyar—was appointed.11 The reason for his appointment was that Abū ʿAwn consulted people about a man to appoint as judge. It is also said that it was Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAlī who consulted them. Three men were recommended to him: Ḥaywah ibn Shurayḥ, Abū Khuzaymah Ibrāhīm ibn Yazīd al-Ḥimyarī, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAyyāsh al-Qitbānī. Abū Khuzaymah was then in Alexandria, and he was summoned from there. Then the three men were brought before him. The first to be questioned was Ḥaywah ibn Shurayḥ, who refused. A sword and a leather mat were called for him. When Ḥaywah saw this, he took out a key he had with him and said, “This is the key to my house. I have longed to meet my Lord.” When they saw how resolute he was, they let him go. Ḥaywah said to them, “Do not reveal to my companions how I refused, lest they do as I did.” Thus Ḥaywah escaped.

He said: I heard my father ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam say that ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak said: “Whenever someone was praised to me for his virtue and I saw him, I found him less than what I had been told of him, except for Ḥaywah ibn Shurayḥ and Ibn ʿAwn.”

He said: Then Abū Khuzayma was summoned and offered the judgeship, but he refused. A sword and a leather mat were brought for him, and the old man’s heart faltered; he could not bear it, so he agreed to accept, and was appointed judge.

Ten dinars were assigned to him each month. He would take no allowance for Friday, saying:

“I am only a hired man of the Muslims; if I do no work for them, I will not take their property.” It used to be said to Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ, “Abū Khuzayma has been appointed judge,” and Ḥaywa would say, “Abū Khuzayma is better than I am; he was tested and proved sound.”

He said: Abū Khuzayma used to make ropes and sell them before he became judge. A man from Alexandria passed by him while he was sitting in the court. The man said, “I shall test Abū Khuzayma.” He stopped before him and said, “Abū Khuzayma, I need a rope for my horse.” Abū Khuzayma went to his house, brought out a rope, sold it to him, and then sat down again.

He said: I heard my father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, say: Abū Kharsha al-Murādī was a friend of Abū Khuzayma. One day he passed him and greeted him, but Abū Khuzayma did not respond as he was accustomed to. Abū Kharsha had been involved in a dispute before him over a wall, and this weighed heavily on Abū Kharsha. He complained about it to one of his relatives, who said to him, “Today is Thursday—or, he said, Monday—and he is fasting. When he has prayed the sunset prayer and gone in, ask permission to see him.” Abū Kharsha did so. He said: I went in to him. Before him was a lentil tharīd. He returned my greeting as he used to, and said, “What brings you here?” Abū Kharsha told him. He said, “I did that only because I feared that your adversary would see me greeting you and that it would weaken some of his argument.” Abū Kharsha said, “Then I call you to witness that the wall is his.”

He said: One of the town’s shaykhs told me that Yazīd ibn Ḥātim, who was then governor of the town, came to Abū Khuzayma at his house. Abū Khuzayma came out to him at the door of his house. A saddle cushion was laid out for Yazīd ibn Ḥātim, and he sat on it until his need was attended to; then he left. Abū Khuzayma was questioned about this, and said, “There was nothing in my house for him to sit on, so I went out to him.”

Aḥmad ibn ʿAmr ibn Sarḥ Abū al-Ṭāhir related to us. He said: Some of the Banū Miskīn brought a matter concerning their detention before Abū Khuzayma. One of the judges had considered it, but Abū Khuzayma seemed unwilling to enforce the ruling. He wrote to him: “If we do not benefit from the opinion of the judges before you when it is brought to you, then we will likewise not benefit from your opinion when it is brought before the judges after you.” So he enforced it.

He said: One day he left the court and did not find his mount. A man from the town—whom I think was Ibn Abī al-Juwayriyya—offered him his mount to ride, but he refused. Another man offered him his mount, and he rode it. The first man questioned him about this. He said, “I refrained from riding it only because I saw two silver cheekpieces on the bridle.”

He said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAyyāsh was appointed to deliver exhortations. ʿUqba ibn Muslim had held that office, but was removed from it. ʿUqba ibn Muslim said, as Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr related to us: “Why have I been dismissed? By God, I am neither in charge of taxation nor of war. I am only a preacher who leads the people in prayer. If I speak at length and they want me to be brief, I am brief; and if I am brief and they want me to speak at length, I speak at length.”

He said: Then Abū Khuzayma asked to be relieved, and was excused. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bilāl al-Ḥaḍramī was appointed in his place.

It is said that it was in fact Ghawth, whom he had appointed as his deputy when Ghawth went to the Commander of the Faithful, Abū Jaʿfar. This was in the year 144. He would hold court for the people in the White Mosque. Then Ghawth returned and confirmed him as his deputy, to judge between the people, until ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bilāl died. When he died, Ghawth rode to his house and took possession of the register, the deposits that had been in his care, and other things. They allege that ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bilāl’s daughter cried out that day, “What humiliation!”

Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr related to us. He said: Abū Khuzayma remained judge until Ghawth returned from the summer campaign. Abū Khuzayma was dismissed, and Ghawth was restored to the judgeship.

It is also said that when Ghawth ibn Sulaymān went to Iraq, he appointed Abū Khuzayma Ibrāhīm ibn Yazīd to the judgeship. He remained judge until he died in the year 154.

Ibn Ḥudayj was in Iraq at the time. He said: I went in to the Commander of the Faithful, Abū Jaʿfar, and he said to me: “Ibn Ḥudayj, a man has died in your town, and the people have suffered a loss through him.” I said, “Commander of the Faithful, that must be Abū Khuzayma.” He said, “Yes. Whom do you think we should appoint judge after him?” I said, “Abū Maʿdān al-Yaḥṣubī, Commander of the Faithful.” He said, “That man is deaf, and a judge should not be deaf.” I said, “Then Ibn Lahīʿa, Commander of the Faithful.” He said, “Ibn Lahīʿa, despite his weakness.” He ordered his appointment and assigned him thirty dinars each month. He was the first judge of Egypt to receive such an allowance, and the first judge there appointed by a caliph. Until then, it had been the governors of the town who appointed the judges. He remained judge until he was dismissed in the year 164.

Ismāʿīl ibn al-Yasaʿ al-Kūfī was appointed and dismissed in the year 167. He was well regarded by the people of the town, except that he followed Abū Ḥanīfa’s doctrine, which the people of the town did not know at that time.

My father, ʿAbd Allāh, related to us. He said: Al-Layth ibn Saʿd wrote about him to the Commander of the Faithful: “Commander of the Faithful, you have appointed over us a man who works to undermine the Sunna of the Messenger of God ﷺ in our midst, although, as far as we know, he has done nothing but good with respect to the dinar and the dirham.” The Commander of the Faithful wrote ordering his dismissal.

Ghawth ibn Sulaymān was restored to the judgeship and remained in office until he died on the first day of Jumādā al-Ākhira in the year 168.

Ḥammād ibn Maswar Abū Rajāʾ related to us. He said: A woman from the countryside came, while Ghawth was judge and riding in a litter. She met Ghawth ibn Sulaymān among the saddlers as he was on his way to the mosque. She complained to him of her situation and told him what she needed. He dismounted in the saddlers’ shops, without reaching the mosque, and wrote out what she needed for her. Then he mounted and went to the mosque. The woman went away saying, “By God, your mother was right to name you Ghawth. You are indeed a succor, just as your name says.”

He said: When Ghawth died, al-Mufaḍḍal ibn Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd al-Qibṭī was appointed judge. He was dismissed in the year 169. He was the first judge in Egypt to make the written records lengthy, and he was one of the most virtuous and finest of people.

He said: One of the town’s shaykhs told me that a man met al-Mufaḍḍal after he had been dismissed and said, “May God be your judge! You ruled against me unjustly and did such-and-such.” Al-Mufaḍḍal said to him, “But the man in whose favor we ruled speaks well of me.”

Then Abū al-Ṭāhir al-Aʿraj appointed ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr ibn Ḥazm al-Anṣārī, who was well regarded during his tenure.

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related to us. He said: The chief of the barīd at that time wrote to him, “You are slow to sit to hear the people’s cases.” Abū al-Ṭāhir wrote back to him: “If the Commander of the Faithful has ordered you to do something, then do so; otherwise, your sleeves, saddlecloths, and your beasts’ backsides will keep you busy enough without attending to the affairs of the people.”

Then he asked to be relieved, and was excused in the year 174. They said to him, “Advise us on a man to appoint.” He recommended al-Mufaḍḍal ibn Faḍāla, and al-Mufaḍḍal ibn Faḍāla was appointed. Abū al-Ṭāhir then went to Iraq and said, “I thought I would be excused from the post. Had I known otherwise, I would not have asked to be relieved of Egypt; it was a good corner.” Al-Mufaḍḍal remained judge until Ṣafar of the year 177.

Muḥammad ibn Masrūq al-Kindī, from among the people of Kūfa, was appointed. His tenure was not well regarded; he was overbearing and tyrannical. He remained judge until the year 184, when he went to Iraq.

Isḥāq ibn al-Furāt al-Tujībī was appointed as his deputy; he was a Ḥimyarī. He remained judge until Ṣafar of the year 185, when he was dismissed.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mujabbir ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb was appointed judge and remained so until he was dismissed in Jumādā al-Ūlā of the year 194. Some people had complained of being wronged by him and had brought their complaint against him to the Commander of the Faithful, Hārūn. He said, “Look in the register: how many governors do I have from the family of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb?” They looked and found no one other than him. He said, “By God, I will never bring him low.”

After him, Hāshim ibn Abī Bakr al-Bakrī, a descendant of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq, was appointed. He harmed the followers of al-ʿUmarī, and they suffered greatly from him. He followed the doctrine of the followers of Abū Ḥanīfa. He remained judge until he died on the first day of Muḥarram in the year 196.

Then Ibrāhīm ibn al-Bakkāʾ was appointed by Jābir ibn al-Ashʿath, who was then governor of the town. He remained in office until Jābir ibn al-Ashʿath was overthrown and removed, and ʿAbbād ibn Muḥammad was appointed in his place; ʿAbbād dismissed Ibn al-Bakkāʾ.

Laḥīʿa ibn ʿĪsā al-Ḥaḍramī was appointed. He remained judge until al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik arrived at the beginning of the year 198 and dismissed Laḥīʿa.

Al-Faḍl ibn Ghānim was appointed. Al-Muṭṭalib had brought him with him from Iraq. He served for a year or so, then al-Muṭṭalib became angry with him and dismissed him.

Laḥīʿa ibn ʿĪsā was appointed. He remained judge until he died on the first day of Dhū al-Qaʿda in the year 204.

After consulting the people of the town, al-Sarī ibn al-Ḥakam appointed Ibrāhīm ibn Isḥāq al-Qārī, a client of Banū Zuhra. The judgeship and the office of preaching were combined for him. He was a man of integrity. Then he asked to be relieved over something he disapproved of, and was excused.

Ibrāhīm ibn al-Jarrāḥ was appointed in his place. He followed the doctrine of the followers of Abū Ḥanīfa and was not blameworthy at the beginning of his tenure, until his son came to him from Iraq. Then his conduct changed and his judgments became corrupt. He remained judge until the year 211, when ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṭāhir entered the town and dismissed him.

ʿĪsā ibn al-Munkadir ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Munkadir was appointed. Ibrāhīm ibn al-Jarrāḥ went to Iraq and died there. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṭāhir assigned ʿĪsā ibn al-Munkadir four thousand dirhams a month. He was the first judge to receive that amount. He also granted him one thousand dinars. When al-Muʿtaṣim came to Egypt in the year 214, Ibn Abī Duʾād spoke to him about ʿĪsā. Al-Muʿtaṣim ordered him to suspend judgment, then later had him summoned to Iraq, where he died.

Egypt remained without a judge until al-Maʾmūn appointed Hārūn ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Zuhrī to the judgeship. He arrived in the town with ten nights remaining in Ramaḍān of the year 217. He was well regarded, upright, and beloved among the people of the town. He remained judge until Rabīʿ al-Awwal of the year 226, when he was instructed to refrain from judging. His position had become burdensome to Ibn Abī Duʾād.

Abū al-Wazīr arrived as governor of Egypt’s land tax, bringing with him the letter appointing Ibn Abī al-Layth to the judgeship. He remained judge until Thursday, when thirteen nights of Shaʿbān had passed, in the year 235; then he was dismissed and imprisoned.

Egypt remained without a judge until al-Ḥārith ibn Miskīn was appointed in Jumādā al-Ūlā of the year 237. His appointment to the judgeship reached him while he was in Alexandria. He remained judge until he was dismissed on Friday, with seven nights remaining in Rabīʿ al-Ākhir of the year 245.

Daḥīm ibn al-Yatīm appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ibrāhīm ibn al-Yatīm al-Dimashqī. His appointment reached him at al-Ramla, but he died before reaching Egypt. He died in the year 245.

After him, Bakkār ibn Qutayba Abū Bakra al-Thaqafī was appointed. He was from among the people of Baṣra and descended from Abū Bakra, the Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ.* He entered the town on Friday, with eight nights of Jumādā al-Ākhira elapsed, in the year 246.

Abū al-Qāsim Ibn Qadīd said: After Bakkār, Egypt remained without a judge until Khumārawayh ibn Aḥmad appointed Muḥammad ibn ʿAbda judge in the year 277. He remained judge until Jumādā al-Ākhira of the year 283. Egypt remained without a judge until Abū Zurʿa Muḥammad ibn ʿUthmān al-Dimashqī was appointed.

Mention of the traditions

He said: This is a list of those among the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ from whom the people of Egypt transmitted: those who entered Egypt and became known to the people of Egypt as authorities for transmission; those from other lands who shared in transmitting from them, and the reports transmitted exclusively by the Egyptians; and those whose entry into Egypt is known through reports transmitted by others from them. I have left out some people whom certain people mention as having been Companions and as having entered Egypt, for I have not found any of their shaykhs among the people of knowledge to affirm this for them. I have omitted much of the material from some of those I have mentioned, to avoid prolixity, and have confined myself to some of it.

ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil al-Sahmī

He was the first commander appointed over the people of Egypt in Islam. They have more than twenty traditions from him. Among them is the report that ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ taught me fifteen prostrations in the Qurʾān: three in the short sūras at the end of the Qurʾān, and two in Sūrat al-Ḥajj.” Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam related it to us, from Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd, from al-Ḥārith ibn Saʿīd al-ʿAtqī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Munayn—of Banū ʿAbd Kulāl—from ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ.

Among them is the report that ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ said: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “Whenever usury becomes prevalent among a people, they are overtaken by famine; whenever fornication becomes prevalent among a people, they are overtaken by drought; and whenever bribery becomes prevalent among a people, they are overtaken by terror”. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sulaymān, that Muḥammad ibn Rāshid al-Murādī told him that ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ once ascended the pulpit and did not give the greeting. A man said, “Abū ʿAbd Allāh is surely angry.” ʿAmr said: “By God, you know that I am among the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ who narrate the fewest reports from him, and nothing kept me from narrating from him except that I was a man much given to campaigning. I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: ‘Whenever a people become prevalent…’” Then he recounted the tradition.

Among them is the tradition of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from ʿImrān ibn Abī Anas, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Jubayr, from ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ sent me on an expedition and put me in command of it. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb was among them. On a bitterly cold night I became ritually impure, so I performed dry ablution and led them in prayer. When we came to the Messenger of God ﷺ ʿUmar complained about me to him, and among the things he said was, “He led us in prayer while in a state of major ritual impurity.” The Messenger of God ﷺ sent for me and asked me about it. I said:

“Messenger of God, I became ritually impure on a cold night unlike any I had ever experienced. I had to choose between washing and dying, or leading them in prayer while ritually impure. So I performed dry ablution and led them in prayer.” The Messenger of God ﷺ said:

“If I had been in your place, I would have done as you did.”

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related it to us in this form, from Ibn Lahīʿa. Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār al-Makhzūmī also related it to us: Zayd ibn al-Ḥubāb related to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from ʿImrān ibn Abī Anas, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Jubayr, from Abū Firās Yazīd ibn Rabāḥ—the client of ʿAmr—from ʿAmr.

Among these is the report of Mūsā ibn ʿAlī, from his father, from Abū Qays, the client of ʿAmr, from ʿAmr: The Messenger of God ﷺ said: “The distinction between our fast and the fast of the People of the Book is the pre-dawn meal” .

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us: Mūsā ibn ʿAlī related it to us, from his father. My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related it to us and said: al-Layth ibn Saʿd related it to us, from Mūsā ibn ʿAlī.

Among these is the report of Mūsā ibn ʿAlī, from his father, from ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ sent for me and said, “Take your clothes and weapons with you.” So I took my clothes and weapons with me, then went to the Messenger of God and found him performing ablution. He looked steadily at me , then lowered his gaze and said, “ʿAmr, I intend to put you in command of an army, through which God will grant you spoils and keep you safe. I have a fine share of wealth in mind for you.” I replied, “By God, Messenger of God, I did not embrace Islam for wealth, but out of desire for Islam and to be with you.” He said, “ʿAmr, indeed, good wealth is excellent for a righteous man” . ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us.

Among these is the report of Mūsā ibn ʿAlī, from his father, who said: I heard ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ say, “How far your way is from the way of your Prophet! He was the most ascetic of people in this world, while you are the most desirous of it.” ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us, from Mūsā ibn ʿAlī.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related to us: al-Layth ibn Saʿd related to us, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, that ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ informed him that he had heard ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ say from the pulpit: “By God, I have never seen a people more desirous of what the Messenger of God ﷺ renounced than you. You have come to desire this world, while the Messenger of God ﷺ renounced it. No three days of time passed for the Messenger of God ﷺ without what he owed being more than what he owned.”

Some of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ said, “We saw the Messenger of God ﷺ borrow.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, who had heard ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, that a client of ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ related to him that ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ said: The Messenger of God ﷺ said, “A deed the size of a barley grain today is better than a qīrāṭ’s weight after today.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among these is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, that Ibn Shamāsa informed him that when ʿAmr was near death, his eyes shed tears. ʿAbd Allāh said to him, “Abū ʿAbd Allāh, is fear of death making you anxious to the point of this?” He said, “No. But what comes after death.” Then ʿAbd Allāh recounted his experiences with the Messenger of God ﷺ and the conquests that took place in Syria . When ʿAbd Allāh had finished, he said, “I have passed through three stages . Had I died in any one of them, I would have known what people would say. When God sent Muḥammad, I was the person most hostile to what he had brought. I wished I could kill him. My hatred of God’s religion went so far that I sailed to the ruler of Abyssinia to seek the blood of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ. Had I died then, people would have said, ‘ʿAmr died an idolater, an enemy of God and His Messenger, one of the people of Hell.’ Then God cast Islam into my heart, and I went to the Messenger of God ﷺ. He held out his hand to me to pledge allegiance, but I withdrew my hand and said, ‘I pledge allegiance to you on condition that God forgive me for my past sins.’ At that time, I thought I would commit no sin in Islam. The Messenger of God ﷺ said, ‘ʿAmr, Islam effaces what came before it, and the Emigration effaces what came between it and Islam’” . “Had I died at this stage, people would have said, ‘ʿAmr embraced Islam and emigrated with the Messenger of God ﷺ; we hope that much good awaits ʿAmr with God.’ Then came governorships and tribulations, and I am apprehensive about this stage.

“When you carry me out, hurry with me, and let no wailing woman or fire follow me. Fasten my waist-wrapper tightly around me, for I shall be disputing. Sprinkle earth over me gently, for my right side has no greater claim to earth than my left. Do not put any piece of wood or brick into the grave. Then, when you have buried me, remain beside me for as long as it takes to slaughter a camel and butcher it, so that I may find comfort in your company.”

Abū Ṣāliḥ ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ and Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us, from al-Layth ibn Saʿd. He said: Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb related to us that Ibn Shamāsa informed him that when ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ was near death—then he mentioned the report.

He said: ʿAmr ibn Sawwād related to us: Ibn Wahb related to us: Ibn Lahīʿa informed us, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Ibn Shamāsa, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, from ʿAmr. He added in it that ʿAmr said to him, “You have left something better than that: the testimony that there is no god but God…”

Asad ibn Mūsā related to us: Ibn Abī Zāʾida related to us: Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb related to me: Suwayd ibn Qays informed me, from Qays ibn Sammī, that ʿAmr said: I said, “Messenger of God, I pledge allegiance to you on condition that He forgive me for my past sins.” The Messenger of God ﷺ said, “Islam effaces what came before it, and the Emigration effaces what came before it.” ʿAmr said : “By God, I was the most bashful of people before the Messenger of God ﷺ . I never looked him in the eye , nor did I address him about what I wanted, until he passed on to God, out of bashfulness before him.” Then he mentioned the report.

Among these is the report of Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Rāshid, the client of Ḥabīb ibn Aws al-Thaqafī, that Ḥabīb related to him, and that ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ related to him: When we returned from the Trench, I gathered a group of Quraysh privately, and said to them, “Know, by God, that I see Muḥammad’s cause rising above all opposing causes in a striking way. Would you consider a plan I have devised?” They said, “What is it?” I said, “Let us go to the Negus and stay with him until matters between us and Muḥammad are settled. If Quraysh prevail, we will return to them; but if Muḥammad prevails, we will stay with him. For I would rather be under the Negus’s authority than under Muḥammad’s.” They said, “You are right.”

“I said, ‘Gather some leather goods for him, for that is the gift from our land he likes best.’” He said: So we did that, then set out. When we had drawn near him, I saw ʿAmr ibn Umayya, whom the Messenger of God ﷺ had sent to the Negus. He said: I thought, “By God, this is ʿAmr ibn Umayya, whom Muḥammad has sent. If I bring my gifts to the Negus and then ask him for this man, and he gives him to me, I will kill him; then Quraysh will see that I have avenged them by killing Muḥammad’s envoy.”

He said: When ʿAmr ibn Umayya had entered before him and finished his business, I entered and greeted him as we used to greet him and the Negus said, “Welcome! What have you brought me, my friend?” I said, “Your Majesty, I have brought you gifts.” Then I presented my gifts to him , and he accepted them. I was delighted by what he had said to me. I said to him, “Your Majesty, I saw at your door an envoy of Muḥammad, who is our enemy. Give him to me so that I may strike off his head, for he is the envoy of a man who is our enemy.” He stretched out his hand, then grew angry and struck me on the nose with it so hard that I thought he had broken it. I wished the earth would split open for me so I could disappear into it, out of fear of him.

“Then he said, ‘You ask me for the envoy of a man who receives the Greatest Spirit, the one who used to come to Moses, so that I might give him to you to kill !’ I said, ‘Your Majesty, is it really so—that he receives the Greatest Spirit that came to Moses?’ He said, ‘Yes, by Him in whose hand is the Negus’s soul. Woe to you, ʿAmr! Obey me and follow him. By Him in whose hand is my soul, he and those who follow him will surely prevail over all others, over those who oppose them , just as Moses prevailed over Pharaoh and his troops.’ I said, ‘Will you pledge allegiance to me on his behalf as a Muslim?’ He said, ‘Yes.’ He held out his hand and pledged allegiance to me on his behalf. I left to join my companions, but my view had changed from what it had been with them.

“He said: I set off, my mount carrying me swiftly, until I met Khālid ibn al-Walīd. I said, ‘Where are you going, Abū Sulaymān?’ He said, ‘By God, I intend to go and embrace Islam. The matter has surely become clear, and the sign has become plain.’ I said, ‘And so do I, by God.’

“He said: We set out until we came to the Messenger of God ﷺ and entered the mosque. Khālid went first and pledged allegiance to him , then I stepped forward and pledged allegiance. I said, ‘Messenger of God, I pledge allegiance to you on condition that He forgive me for my past sins’; I did not mention what was to come. The Messenger of God ﷺ said, ‘Pledge allegiance, ʿAmr, for Islam effaces what came before it, and the Emigration effaces what came before it.’

Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us: Yaḥyā ibn Abī Zāʾida related it to us, from Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq.

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Hishām related it to us, from Ziyād ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bakkāʾī, from Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq.

ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ died on the day of Fiṭr in the year forty-three. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr performed the funeral prayer over him, and he was buried at al-Muqaṭṭam, in the direction of al-Fajj. He was called Abū ʿAbd Allāh. The road to the Ḥijāz ran by there at that time , and he wished those who passed by would pray for him. Ibn ʿUfayr informed us of this.

ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related to us: Ibn Lahīʿa related to us and said: Five of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ who are known, were buried in the cemetery of al-Muqaṭṭam: ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ al-Sahmī, ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ al-Zubaydī, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥudhāfa al-Sahmī, Abū Baṣra al-Ghifārī, and ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir al-Juhanī.

Among the people of Medina who shared with the people of Egypt in transmitting reports from him was Qabīṣa ibn Dhuʾayb. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: He was born in the year of the Conquest. Also among them were Abū Murra, the client of ʿUqayl ibn Abī Ṭālib, whose name was Yazīd; ʿUrwa ibn al-Zubayr—though there was disagreement about Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab: some said he heard from him, while others said he only heard from his son ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr—and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sharāḥīl. Among the people of Kūfa was Qays ibn Abī Ḥāzim.

Among the people of Baṣra were Abū ʿUthmān al-Nahdī and others.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ

They have approximately one hundred reports from him. Among them is the report of Rajāʾ ibn Abī ʿAṭāʾ al-Maʿāfirī, from Wāhib ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Maʿāfirī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said:

“Whoever gives his brother bread until he is satisfied , and gives him water until his thirst is quenched , God will distance him from the Fire by seven trenches, the distance between each two trenches being a journey of five hundred years.” Idrīs ibn Yaḥyā and ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Wāhib ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Maghāfirī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, that he saw in a dream that he was holding honey on one of his fingers and ghee on another, and it was as though he were licking them.

In the morning he told the Messenger of God ﷺ about it. He said, “If you live, you will read the two scriptures, the Torah and the Criterion.” And he did read them. Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us.

Among these is the report of al-Layth ibn ʿĀmir ibn Yaḥyā, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥubulī, who said:

I heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr say: The Messenger of God ﷺ said, “A man from my community will be called out before all creatures, and ninety-nine scrolls will be spread out before him , each scroll as far as the eye can see. Then God will say to him, ‘Do you deny anything in this? Have My recording angels wronged you?’ He will say, ‘No, my Lord.’ He will say, ‘Do you have an excuse?’ The man will be daunted [by this] and say, ‘No, my Lord.’ He will say, ‘Indeed, you have with Us two good deeds, and you will not be wronged.’ Then a small card will be brought out for him, on which is written, ‘I testify that there is no god but God, and that Muḥammad is God’s servant and messenger.’ He will say, ‘My Lord, what is this card compared with these scrolls?’ It will be said, ‘You will not be wronged.’ The scrolls will then be placed on one side of the balance and the card on the other; the scrolls will rise up, and the card will weigh heavily .” Thus he will be saved from the Fire.

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

My father related to us: Bakr ibn Muḍar related to us, from ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith, from ʿĀmir ibn Yaḥyā, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥubulī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, who said: On the Day of Resurrection, a servant will be brought with ninety-nine scrolls of sins and misdeeds, and he will be ordered to the Fire. When he is taken away, a caller will call out, “Do not hurry! He still has something left.” Then a small card will be brought, and on it will be written, “There is no god but God…”

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Sharāḥīl ibn Yazīd, who said: There was a dispute between Ḥanash ibn ʿAbd Allāh and me, and he said, “Were it not for something I heard from Ibn ʿAmr, I would have known [uncertain reading] I heard him say: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say, ‘Three things: if I do them, I do not care what I have done—if I compose poetry, wear an amulet, or drink an antidote.’” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us. Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ also transmitted it, from Sharāḥīl ibn Yazīd.

Among these is the report of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAyyāsh, from his father, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥubulī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Whoever is given knowledge and conceals it, God will bridle him on the Day of Resurrection with a bridle of fire” . Idrīs ibn Yaḥyā related it to us.

Among these is the report of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ziyād ibn Anʿum, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “God will surely strengthen Islam through men who are not of its people” . Al-Muqriʾ related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū Zurʿa, from Ibn ʿAmr, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said:

“The Hour will not come until the Qurʾān and the remembrance—or the Cornerstone—are taken up” . ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam was uncertain . ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

Among these is the report of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ziyād ibn Anʿum, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Rāfiʿ al-Tanūkhī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Knowledge consists of three things; anything beyond that is additional: a clear verse, an established practice, or an equitable obligation” . Muʿādh ibn al-Ḥakam related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥasan ibn Thawbān al-Hawzanī, from Hishām ibn Abī Ruqayya al-Lakhmī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “There is no ill omen from birds, no contagion, no owl, and no jadd; and the evil eye is real” . Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among these is the report of Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd and Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū Hānīʾ al-Khawlānī, who heard Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥubulī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “God recorded the decrees of all creatures fifty thousand years before He created the heavens and the earth, while His Throne was upon the water” . Abū Ṣadaqa Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Aʿlā related it to us, from Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd. Abū al-Aswad related a report to us from Ibn Lahīʿa ; one of their two reports is similar to the other.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related to us, from al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Abū Hānīʾ al-Khawlānī, with an isnād similar to their two reports.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū Hānīʾ, who heard Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥubulī say:

He heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr say that he heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “No force that goes forth fighting in God’s path and acquires spoils fails thereby to receive in advance two-thirds of its reward in the Hereafter, with one-third remaining for it; but if it acquires no spoils, its reward is made complete” . Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among these is the report of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ziyād ibn Anʿum, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yaʿqūb, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “God is more protective of a believer’s blood than any of you is of his precious property, until He takes him while he is on his bed” . Al-Muqriʾ related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Suwayd ibn Qays, who informed him, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “A day spent guarding the frontier in God’s path is better than a month of fasting and prayer” . My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among these is the report of Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb, from Abū Qabīl, who related to him that he was with ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, and we were discussing the conquest of Constantinople and Rome—whichever of the two would be conquered first. ʿAbd Allāh called for a large chest of his. We asked, “What does ‘large’ mean?” He said, “With a ring.” He said, “We used to sit with the Messenger of God ﷺ writing down what he said, whether yes or no. We asked, ‘Which of the two cities will be conquered first, Messenger of God?’ He said, ‘The city of Heraclius’” —meaning Constantinople. Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr related it to us.

Ibn Lahīʿa contradicted Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb in this report; God knows best what is correct.

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us: Ibn Lahīʿa related to us, from Abū Qabīl, from ʿUmayr ibn Mālik, that he was with Ibn ʿAmr, and they mentioned the conquest of Constantinople and Rome, asking which of the two would be conquered first;

They differed about this. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr called for a chest containing sheets and said: “You will conquer Constantinople, then you will send an expedition against Rome, and God will grant you its conquest. Otherwise, I am one of the liars in God’s sight.”

Among them is the ḥadīth of Qabāth ibn Razīn, from a shaykh of the Maʿāfir—who is described as possessing merit and piety—that a man called ʿAbbād, one of those who kept company with ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, was among the righteous. He would recite the Qurʾān, joining sūras together in a single rakʿa. This reached ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr. One day ʿAbbād came to him, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr said to him, “O betrayer of his trust!” three times. This weighed heavily on ʿAbbād, so he said to him, “May God forgive you! What trust have you heard that I betrayed?” He said, “Have I not been told that you join sūras together in a single rakʿa?” He replied, “I do that.” He said, “Then what will you do when each sūra demands its own rakʿa and its two prostrations from you? I only said this to you as the Messenger of God ﷺ—said it to me.” ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us.

Among them is the ḥadīth of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ḥayy ibn ʿAbd Allāh, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥublī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—went out on the day of the Trench while they were digging around the city. The Messenger of God took up the pickaxe and struck a blow with it. He said, “With this blow, God will open the treasures of the Romans.” Then he struck a second time and said, “With this, He will open the treasures of Persia.” Then he struck a third time and said:

“With this blow, God will bring the people of Yemen as helpers and supporters.” ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

Among them is the ḥadīth of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn ʿAmr al-Maʿāfirī, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥublī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, that the Messenger of God said: “Whoever is silent is saved.” Al-Muqriʾ and Abū al-Aswad related it to us.

Among them is the ḥadīth of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Hubayra, from Abū Hubayra al-Kaḥlānī—a client of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr—from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, that one day the Messenger of God ﷺ—came out to them in the mosque and said: “My Lord has forbidden me wine, games of chance, fermented millet drink, the drum, and the qannīn.” Ṭalq ibn al-Samḥ al-Lakhmī related it to us.

Among them is the ḥadīth of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ḥayy ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Maʿāfirī, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥublī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—went out on the day of Badr with three hundred and fifteen fighters, just as Ṭālūt had gone out. As they set out, he prayed for them: “O God, they are barefoot, so provide them with mounts. O God, they are unclothed, so clothe them. O God, they are hungry, so feed them.” God granted them victory on the day of Badr, and they returned with not a single man among them who did not have the head of one or two camels in his hand. They were clothed and had eaten their fill.

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

Among them is the ḥadīth of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAyyāsh al-Qitbānī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿIyāḍ, from Abū Razīn al-Ghāfiqī, who said: I heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr say: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ—say:

“Whoever deliberately passes in front of his brother while he is praying will wish on the Day of Resurrection that he had been a withered tree.” Idrīs ibn Yaḥyā related it to us.

Among them is the ḥadīth of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAyyāsh, from ʿĪsā ibn Hilāl al-Ṣadafī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, that a man came to the Messenger of God ﷺ—and said, “Messenger of God, teach me to recite.” He said, “Recite three from the sūras beginning ‘Alif Lām Rāʾ.’” The man said, “Messenger of God, I am advanced in age, my bones are weak, and my tongue is heavy.” He said, “Recite three from the Ḥā Mīm sūras.” The man said the same. He said, “Recite three from the Sabbih sūras.” The man said the same. Then the Messenger of God ﷺ—said, “Recite,” and he had him recite “When the earth is shaken.”

When he had finished, the man said, “Messenger of God, teach me something I can put into practice.” He said, “The five prayers, pilgrimage to the House, fasting in Ramaḍān, giving alms, enjoining what is right, and forbidding what is wrong.” When the man turned away, the Messenger of God ﷺ—said, “Bring the man to me.” When he was brought back, he said, “I have been commanded to observe the Sacrifice as a festival, which God has appointed for this community.” The man said, “What do you say if I have nothing but a sheep belonging to my household?” The Messenger of God ﷺ—said, “Trim your moustache, clip your nails, and shave your pubic hair; that completes your sacrifice before God.” Idrīs ibn Yaḥyā related it to us.

Al-Muqriʾ related it to us: Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb related it to us: ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿAbbās related to me, from ʿĪsā ibn Hilāl, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, from the Messenger of God ﷺ—a report similar to it.

Among them is the ḥadīth of al-Mufaḍḍal ibn Faḍāla and Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd, from Rabīʿa ibn Sayf, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥublī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, who said: We buried someone with the Messenger of God ﷺ When we returned and he came level with his door, there was a woman approaching. We did not think he recognized her. He said, “Fāṭima, where have you come from?” She said, “I came from the family of this deceased man; I showed compassion to them over their dead and consoled them.” He said:

“Perhaps you went as far as al-Kudā with them.” She said, “God forbid that I should go as far as al-Kudā with them, when I have heard you speak of them as you do.” He said, “Had you gone as far as al-Kudā with them, you would not see Paradise until your grandfather, your father’s father, saw it.”

In Nāfiʿ’s version of the ḥadīth, it says: “until your father’s grandfather saw it.” Al-Kudā means the cemeteries. Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam related it to us, from Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd.

He said: My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ also related it to us, from al-Mufaḍḍal ibn Faḍāla.

Those who shared in transmitting from him were, among the people of Medina: Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab and Abū Salama ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān; among the people of Mecca: ʿAmr ibn Aws al-Thaqafī, Yūsuf ibn Māhak, and Ibn Abī Mulayka; and among the people of Kūfa: Masrūq ibn al-Ajdaʿ, Khaythama ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, and ʿĀmir al-Shaʿbī.

Khārija ibn Ḥudhāfa al-ʿAdawī

They have from him one ḥadīth from the Prophet ﷺ—and no other. It is the ḥadīth of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rāshid al-Zawfī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Murra al-Zawfī, from Khārija ibn Ḥudhāfa, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—came out to us and said:

“God has provided you with an additional prayer that is better for you than red camels: the witr prayer. He has appointed it for you between the night prayer and the rising of dawn.” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us.

My father also related it to us from Bakr ibn Muḍar, from Khālid ibn Yazīd, from Abū al-Ḍaḥḥāk ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Murra, from Khārija ibn Ḥudhāfa.

They also have reports about him himself. Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda and al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Jubayr, that he saw Khārija ibn Ḥudhāfa, a Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ—wiping over his leather socks.

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us. No one other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

Busr ibn Abī Arṭāt, though they sometimes say Busr ibn Arṭāt al-ʿĀmirī

They have from him one ḥadīth from the Prophet ﷺ—and no other. It is the ḥadīth of Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿAbbās, from Shuyaym ibn Baytān, from Junāda ibn Abī Umayya, from Busr ibn Abī Arṭāt, who heard the Messenger of God ﷺ—say: “Hands are not to be amputated during a military expedition.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us. He said: They related it to us.

They also have reports about him himself. Among them is the ḥadīth of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, who said: When Busr rode the sea, he would say, “You are a sea and I am Busr; obedience to God is incumbent on me and on you. Sail under God’s blessing.”

Yūnus ibn Maysara transmitted from him among the people of Syria. No one other than the people of Egypt and the people of Syria transmitted from him. He was known by the kunya Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān and died in Syria during the days of Muʿāwiya.

Al-Mustawrid ibn Shaddād al-Fihrī

They have from him six ḥadīths from the Messenger of God, or something like that. Among them is the ḥadīth of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn ʿAmr al-Maʿāfirī, who said: I heard Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd al-Ḥublī say: I heard al-Mustawrid ibn Shaddād say: I saw the Messenger of God ﷺ—rubbing with his little finger between his toes while performing ablution at al-Juḥfa. My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr, and Abū al-Aswad related it to us; one of them adds a word or something similar.

Among them is the ḥadīth of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Jubayr, from al-Mustawrid ibn Shaddād, who said: While I was in a gathering in which ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ was present, I said, “I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ—say: ‘The people who will be most severe against you are your sister’s sons, the Byzantines; their destruction comes only with the Hour.’” ʿAmr said, “Did I not forbid you to say this?” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār, and ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Ḥudayj ibn Abī ʿAmr, who said:

I heard al-Mustawrid ibn Shaddād say: “I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: ‘Every community has a term, and my community has a term of one hundred years. When one hundred years have passed for my community, what it was promised will come to it.’” ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from Hāniʾ ibn Muʿāwiya al-Ṣadafī, from al-Mustawrid ibn Shaddād, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Do not ask about one who dies while associating partners with God; do not ask about one who dies having deliberately killed a believer; and do not ask about one who dies in disobedience.” Bakr said: Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥublī also related to me, from al-Mustawrid ibn Shaddād, from the Messenger of God ﷺ with this wording, except that he said, “There is hope for him.”

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Jubayr, from al-Mustawrid ibn Shaddād, who said: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “Whoever is entrusted with an office for us, but has no servant, let him acquire a servant; whoever has no dwelling, let him acquire a dwelling; and whoever has no mount, let him acquire a mount. Whoever obtains anything beyond that is an embezzler or a thief.” ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

Among those from Kūfa who shared in transmitting reports from him was Qays ibn Abī Ḥāzim—who is also said to be Abū Isḥāq al-Hamdānī. Only the people of Egypt and Kūfa transmitted from him.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Sarḥ al-ʿĀmirī

He was appointed governor of the province during the caliphate of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān, and the governorship was combined in his person. They have from him, from the Prophet ﷺ one report: the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, who said: ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿAbbās al-Qitbānī related to us, from al-Haytham ibn Shaf, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Sarḥ, who said: While the Messenger of God ﷺ and ten of his Companions were with him—Abū Bakr, ʿUmar, ʿUthmān, ʿAlī, al-Zubayr, and others—on a mountain, the mountain moved beneath them. The Messenger of God ﷺ said to it: “Be still, Ḥirāʾ, for upon you are none but a prophet, a man of truth, or a martyr.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

They have no report from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ other than this one. There is another report with a broken chain, transmitted with uncertainty. It is the report of Ḍimām ibn Ismāʿīl, from ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿAbbās al-Qitbānī, who said: When they laid siege to Alexandria, the commander of the vanguard said to them: “Do not hurry until I have ordered you what I think best.” When the gate was opened, two men entered and were killed. The commander of the vanguard wept. Ḍimām said: “I think he was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd.” He was asked: “Why did you weep when they were martyrs?” He replied: “Would that they were martyrs! But I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: ‘A disobedient person does not enter Paradise.’ I had ordered that they not enter, but they went in without permission.” ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

They also have from him accounts concerning his own affairs. Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Abī Jaʿfar, from Abū Saʿīd al-Ghāfiqī, who heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Sarḥ say from the pulpit: “Do not give your beasts wine to drink, for it is filth from Satan’s work.” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, who said: Al-ʿAlawī related to me, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rabīʿa, who said: We campaigned with ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd in Ifrīqiya, and he led them in a prayer. While they were praying, the people panicked and broke off. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd said to them: “This prayer has been interrupted; repeat your prayer.” He led them in the prayer again, and they repeated it. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related to us; Bakr ibn Muḍar related to us, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Qays ibn Abī Yazīd, from al-Jallās ibn ʿĀmir, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rabīʿa, who said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd led the people in the sunset prayer in Ifrīqiya. When he had prayed two units, a commotion was heard in the mosque. They were terrified by it and thought the enemy had come, so he broke off the prayer. When he saw that there was nothing there, he addressed the people and said: “This prayer has been interrupted.” He ordered his muezzin to call the prayer, then he repeated it.

Only the people of Egypt transmitted from him. He died at ʿAsqalān during the days of Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān, before the people united behind him. His kunya was Abū Yaḥyā. It is also said that ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd died in the year thirty-six. He was governor of Egypt after ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ.

Among those of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ who entered it, shared in transmitting reports to its people, and introduced unusual reports among them—

al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām

They have from him one report. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from someone who heard ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra say: I heard Sufyān ibn Wahb al-Khawlānī say: “When we conquered Egypt without a treaty, al-Zubayr stood up and said: ‘Divide it, ʿAmr!’ ʿAmr replied: ‘I will not divide it until I consult the Commander of the Faithful.’ Al-Zubayr said: ‘By God, you shall divide it, just as the Messenger of God ﷺ divided Khaybar.’ ʿAmr replied:

‘By God, I will not divide it until I consult the Commander of the Faithful.’ So he wrote to ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, and ʿUmar wrote back to him: ‘Leave it as it is until a generation is born from it that is itself a generation’s offspring.’” Yūsuf ibn ʿAdī related it to us, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak. He said: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama also related it to us.

Ibn Lahīʿa said: Yaḥyā ibn Maymūn also related to me, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra, from Sufyān ibn Wahb, with a similar report.

He died at Wādī al-Sibāʿ in the year thirty-six; Ibn Jurmūz killed him. His kunya was Abū ʿAbd Allāh.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb

They have from him something like eight reports, all of them unusual. Among them is the report of Abū Shurayḥ ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Shurayḥ, from Sharāḥīl ibn Bukayl, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar, who said: I was with the Messenger of God ﷺ when the prohibition of wine was revealed. He ordered that the wine vessels be gathered together in one place. Then, the next morning, the Messenger of God ﷺ set out holding my left hand in his right. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb came, so he moved me to his left and the Messenger of God ﷺ held my right hand in his left. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb held my left hand in his right. We walked with the Messenger of God ﷺ between us. Abū Bakr came, so the Messenger of God ﷺ released my hand and moved ʿUmar to his left. He took Abū Bakr’s left hand in his right hand and we walked until we came to the vessels that had been gathered, containing wine and wine skins. He said: “Bring me a blade or a knife.” The Messenger of God ﷺ bared his forearms and took the knife. ʿUmar and Abū Bakr said: “Messenger of God, we will do it for you.” He said: “Break them, because of the anger of God they contain. Wine is forbidden. God has cursed the one who drinks it, serves it, sells it, buys it, carries it, has it carried to him, presses it, has it pressed, oversees it, or consumes its price.” Ṭalq ibn al-Samḥ related it to us.

He said: My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār, and ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related to us; they said: Ibn Lahīʿa related to us, from Abū Ṭaʿma, who said: I heard Ibn ʿUmar mention something similar from the Messenger of God ﷺ.

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama said: Ibn Lahīʿa said: Abū Ṭaʿma was the first to teach the people of Egypt to recite.

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related to us; they said: al-Layth ibn Saʿd related to us. My father said: Ibn Lahīʿa also related to me, from Khālid ibn Yazīd, who heard Thābit ibn Yazīd al-Khawlānī mention that he had an uncle who sold wine and traded in it. I made the pilgrimage and went to ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās and told him about it. He said: “People of Muḥammad, had there been a Scripture after your Scripture or a prophet after your Prophet, it would have been revealed to you as it was revealed to those before you. Instead, it has been deferred for you until the Day of Resurrection. It is no lighter upon you: it is forbidden, and its price is forbidden.”

Then I went to Ibn ʿUmar and told him the same thing. He said: “I will tell you about wine. The prohibition of wine was revealed to the Messenger of God ﷺ while I was with him. He said: ‘Whoever has any of it, let him inform me.’ Whenever someone came to him to report that he had some, [he] said: ‘The valley’—until, when it had all gathered there, he went to it. Abū Bakr and ʿUmar came, and he walked between them. When he stood before it, he said: ‘Do you recognize this?’ They said: ‘Yes, this is wine.’ He said: ‘God has cursed wine, the one who drinks it, serves it, presses it, has it pressed, carries it, has it carried to him, sells it, buys it, and consumes its price.’” Al-Layth said: Then he called for a knife and said: “Move away from them.” They did so, then the Prophet ﷺ took it and tore open the wine skins. The people said: “There is in these wine skins—”

“...the benefit,” he said. “Yes, but I do that only because of the displeasure of God.” ʿUmar said, “I will spare you the trouble, Messenger of God.” He said, “No.”

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Qayṣar, the client of Tujīb, from Ibn ʿUmar: He was with the Messenger of God ﷺ when an old man came to him and said, “May I kiss while fasting?” He said, “Yes.” Then, before he had risen from his seat, a young man came and asked him. He said, “No.” Some of us looked at one another, and he said:

“I know why some of you looked at one another: the old man can control himself.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related this to us. Asad ibn Mūsā differed in this report, saying: “ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr.” God knows best.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam said: “It is as though I saw the Egyptians saying that it was Ibn ʿUmar. Qayṣar, the client of Tujīb, was Qayṣar ibn Abī Baḥriyya.”

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū Ṭuʿma, who said: I was with Ibn ʿUmar when a man came to him and asked him about fasting while traveling. He said, “Do not fast.” The man said, “I am able to do so.” Ibn ʿUmar said, “I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: ‘Whoever does not accept God’s concession will bear a sin as great as the mountains of ʿArafāt.’” Al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related this to us.

Ibn ʿUmar had witnessed the conquest with ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ. He died in the year seventy-three. He was known by the teknonym Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān.

Al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad witnessed Badr.

And [the reading is corrupt] three reports about himself are transmitted from him—and they have nothing from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ.
