The first is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, who heard him mention that al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad had campaigned in Ifrīqiya with ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd. When they returned, ʿAbd Allāh said to al-Miqdād, in a house he had built, “What do you think of the construction of this house?” Al-Miqdād said to him, “If it is from the wealth of God, then you have squandered it; and if it is from your own wealth—”

“—then you have been wasteful.” ʿAbd Allāh said, “Were it not for fear that someone would say I had squandered it twice over, I would demolish it.” ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related this to us.

The second is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿAbbās al-Qatabānī, from Abū al-Maʿārik al-Waddānī: A man of Ghāfiq was owed one hundred dinars by a man of Mahra, in the time of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān. They acquired good booty, and the debtor said, “I will pay you ninety dinars now, and you will cancel the hundred for me.” The debt had been deferred. The man of Ghāfiq agreed. Al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad passed by them, and they took hold of the bridle of his mount so that he might witness for them. When they told him the story, he said, “Both of you have declared war against God and His Messenger.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related this to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, who said: Azhar ibn Yazīd al-Ghuṭayfī told me: “On the day of the battle of Jarjīr, Sharīk ibn Sumayy was in charge of dividing the people’s shares. He sold gold dust for gold, some of which was of better quality than the rest. Then they met al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad and told him about it. Al-Miqdād said, ‘This is not permissible.’”

He was known by the teknonym Abū Maʿbad. He died in the year thirty-three, and ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān led the prayer over him.

Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān

They have two reports from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ. The first is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Kaʿb ibn ʿAlqama, who said: Ḥassān ibn Kurayb al-Ḥimyarī informed us: I heard Ibn Dhī al-Kalāʿ say: I heard Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān say that the Messenger of God ﷺ said, “Leave the Turks alone so long as they leave you alone.” Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr related this to us.

The second is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd and Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Suwayd ibn Qays, from Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj, who heard Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān say: I asked Umm Ḥabība, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ “Did the Messenger of God ﷺ pray in the garment in which he had intercourse with her?” One of them said:

“in which he lay beside her.” “She said, ‘Yes, if there was no impurity on it.’” My father, Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related this to us, from al-Layth ibn Saʿd.

He said: My father and ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related this to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa.

My father and Isḥāq ibn Bakr ibn Muḍar related this to us, from Bakr ibn Muḍar, from Jaʿfar ibn Rabīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Suwayd ibn Qays, from Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj, from Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān, with the same wording.

Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān entered Egypt in the year thirty-seven, reaching Salmant in the district of ʿAyn Shams. He was known by the teknonym Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān. He died in Damascus in the year sixty.

Among what demonstrates that Muʿāwiya entered Egypt is the report ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yūsuf related to us. He said: Muḥammad ibn al-Muhājir related to us, from al-ʿAbbās ibn Sālim, from Mudrik ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Azdī—or Abū Mudrik—who said:

“We campaigned in Egypt with Muʿāwiya. We stopped at a place, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr said to Muʿāwiya, ‘Will you permit me to address the people?’ He gave him permission. ʿAbd Allāh stood leaning on his bow , praised God and extolled Him, then said: ‘I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “In my sleep I saw that the pillar of the Book was carried from beneath my head. I followed it with my eyes, and there it was, like a column of light, being taken toward Syria. Indeed, when tribulations arise, faith will be in Syria.”’” He said this three times.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq

They have one report from him: the report of Ibn Lahīʿa and ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from Abū Thawr, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Alms are not lawful for a person of means.” ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir

They have one report from him, namely that of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū ʿAshāna al-Muhabī, of Maʿāfir, who said: I heard ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir say: “Rejoice! By God, you love the Messenger of God ﷺ more than the generality of those who saw him, though you never saw him.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related this to us.

He died in the year thirty-seven. He was known by the teknonym Abū al-Yaqẓān. He entered Egypt during the time of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān, as ʿAbd al-Ḥamīd ibn al-Walīd Abū Zayd Kabid related to us.

Some people have related: “I heard ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir at Dhū al-Ṣawārī.”

Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī witnessed Badr and al-ʿAqaba; his name was Khālid ibn Zayd.

They have nine reports from him that they alone transmit—except for one report, the report about onions, which others transmit along with them. Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, who said: Abū ʿImrān Aslam informed me that he heard Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī say: The Messenger of God ﷺ said to us while we were in Medina, after being told that Abū Sufyān’s caravan was approaching, “Would you be willing to set out and intercept this caravan, so that perhaps God may grant it to us as booty?” We said, “Yes.” We set out, and after we had traveled a day or two, he said to us, “What do you think of the people? They have been informed of your departure.” We said, “By God, Messenger of God, we have no strength to fight the enemy; we only wanted the caravan.” Then he said, “What do you think about fighting the enemy?” We said, “We have no strength to fight them.” Al-Miqdād ibn ʿAmr said, “We will not say what the people of Moses said: ‘Go forth, you and your Lord, and fight; we will stay here.’” Abū Ayyūb said: We, the company of the Anṣār, wished that we had said what al-Miqdād said; it would have been dearer to us than possessing great wealth. Then God sent down to His Messenger ﷺ: “Just as your Lord brought you forth from your home in truth, while a group of the believers were averse to it,” through His words, “while they looked on.”

Then God sent down: “I am with you, so make firm those who believe,” through His words, “every fingertip.” And He said: “When God promised you that one of the two groups would be yours, and you wished that the one without arms would be yours.” The armed group meant evil; the group without arms was the caravan.

When God promised us one of the two groups—the caravan or the people—our hearts were reassured. Then the Messenger of God ﷺ sent a man to look. The man returned and said, “I saw a dark mass, but I do not know...” The Messenger of God ﷺ said, “They are indeed the ones.” He ordered us to count ourselves, and we did so. There were three hundred and thirteen of us. We informed the Messenger of God ﷺ of our number, and he was pleased, praised God, and said, “The number of the companions of Ṭālūt.”

Then we met the people and formed our ranks. An impulse came over us, and Ibn Rawāḥa said, “Messenger of God, I wish to offer you advice, though the Messenger of God is greater than one who needs advice. God is too exalted for His promise to be doubted—”

“—O Ibn Rawāḥa, do not doubt God’s promise. God does not break His promise.”

The Messenger of God ﷺ took a handful of dust and threw it into the faces of the people, and they were routed. Then God, Mighty and Exalted, sent down: “You did not throw when you threw, but God threw.” We killed and captured them. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:

“There must be no captives, for we are only callers” . We, the company of the Anṣār, said, “ʿUmar has said this only out of envy of us.” The Messenger of God ﷺ slept, then awoke and said, “Call ʿUmar for me.” He was called, and the Messenger said to him, “God has sent down: ‘It is not for a prophet to have captives until he has thoroughly subdued [his enemies] in the land,’” to the end of the verse. My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related this to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Aslam Abū ʿImrān, from Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī, who said: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say, “Hasten to perform the sunset prayer before the stars appear.” ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related this to us.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd al-Muqriʾ related to us: Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ related to us: Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb informed us, saying: Abū ʿImrān al-Tujībī told me that ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir performed the sunset prayer late, while we were in Constantinople, and Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī was with us. Abū Ayyūb said to him, “ʿUqba, are you delaying the sunset prayer this much, when you are a Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ? Someone who did not accompany him may see you and think that this is its proper time!” Abū ʿImrān said: I asked Abū Ayyūb, “Then when is its time?” He said, “We used to pray it when the sun had set, hastening to do so before the stars appeared.”

Among these is the report of al-Layth and Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, who said: Aslam Abū ʿImrān told me: We were in Constantinople . ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, a Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ was over the people of Egypt, and Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd was over the people of Syria. A great line of Romans came out from the city and we formed a great line of Muslims against them. One Muslim charged the Romans and entered their midst, then came back to us. The people cried out, “Glory be to God! He has cast himself into destruction!” Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī stood and said, “People, you are interpreting this verse in this way, but this verse was revealed concerning us, the company of the Anṣār. When God strengthened His religion and increased its supporters, we said privately to one another, without the Messenger of God knowing: ‘Our property has been lost. If only we stayed with it and restored what had been lost.’ Then God, Mighty and Exalted, sent down in His Book a reply to what we had resolved to do: ‘Spend in the way of God, and do not cast yourselves into destruction with your own hands.’” Destruction, then, was for us to stay with our property and tend to it. We were commanded to campaign, and Abū Ayyūb continued campaigning in the way of God until God took him. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related this to us, from al-Layth ibn Saʿd. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd al-Muqriʾ related it to us from Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ.

Among these is the report of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ziyād ibn Anʿum, from his father, who said: Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī and I were together at an anchorage on the sea. When our meal was ready, we sent for Abū Ayyūb and the people aboard his vessel. Abū Ayyūb came to us and said, “You invited me, though I am fasting. It was my duty to answer you. I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: ‘A Muslim has six obligatory claims upon his fellow Muslim. Whoever neglects one of them has neglected a duty owed to his brother: when he invites him, he should answer him; when he meets him, he should greet him; when he sneezes, he should invoke a blessing for him; when he falls ill, he should visit him; when he dies, he should follow his funeral procession; and when he seeks his advice, he should advise him.’” Al-Muqriʾ related this to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ḥayy ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Maʿāfirī, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥublī, from Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī, who said: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say, “Whoever separates a mother from her child, God will separate him from his loved ones on the Day of Resurrection.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related this to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Hubayra, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, who said: Abū Ayyūb brought the Messenger of God ﷺ a bowl containing onions. He said, “Eat,” but he refused to eat them and said, “I am not like you.” Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān claimed that Abū Ayyūb did not eat onions, raw or cooked.

He died in Constantinople in the year fifty-one, campaigning with Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya.

ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit had witnessed Badr and al-ʿAqaba.

They have reports from him that they alone transmit. Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa and Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd, from Sayyār ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from Yazīd ibn Qūdar, from Salama ibn Shurayḥ, from ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ enjoined seven things upon us. He said: “Do not associate anything with God, even if you are cut to pieces, burned, or killed; do not deliberately abandon the prescribed prayer—for whoever deliberately abandons it has left the religion; do not commit acts of disobedience, for they incur the displeasure of God; do not drink wine, for it is the root of all sins; do not flee from killing or death, even if you are in the midst of it; do not disobey your parents, even if they command you to leave the whole world—then leave it; do not lay down your staff against your household, and deal justly with them as you would with yourself.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related this to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa and Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam, from Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, who said: ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ told me that he heard Junāda ibn Abī Umayya say: I heard ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit say: A man came to the Messenger of God ﷺ and said, “Messenger of God, which deed is best?” He said, “Faith in God, affirming the truth, and striving in His way.” The man said, “I want something easier than that, Messenger of God.” He said, “Generosity and patience.” The man said, “I want something easier than that.” He said, “Do not suspect God in anything He has decreed for you.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr related this to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Jaʿfar, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥublī, from ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit: The Messenger of God ﷺ said, “No soul that has good with God, when it dies, would wish to return to you—except the martyr, who wishes to return and be killed once more.” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related this to us.

Others also transmit a report from ʿUbāda, namely the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, from al-Ṣunābiḥī, from ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit, who said: “I was one of the leaders who pledged allegiance to the Messenger of God ﷺ. He said, ‘We pledged allegiance to him that we would not associate anything with God, steal, commit fornication, kill a soul God has forbidden [us to kill], or plunder; and we did not stipulate Paradise if we did any of these things or committed any of them. The decision in that matter would rest with God.’” ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related this to us.

(3 He said: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Hishām related to us: Ziyād ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bakkāʾī related to us, from Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, who said: Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb told me, from Murthid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Yazanī, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAsīla al-Ṣunābiḥī, from ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit, who said: I was among those present at the first ʿAqaba, and we were twelve men. We pledged allegiance to the Messenger of God ﷺ in the pledge of the women—this was before fighting was prescribed—that we would not associate anything with God, steal, commit fornication, kill our children, bring forth a slander that we had fabricated between our hands and feet, or disobey him in what was right. If you fulfill this, Paradise is yours; but if you commit any of these things, your affair is in God’s hands: if He wills, He punishes; and if He wills, He forgives. 3)

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: Ibn Shihāb al-Zuhrī also transmitted it, from ʿĀʾidh Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh Abū Idrīs al-Khawlānī, from ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related this to us, from al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from ʿUqayl ibn Khālid; and ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Hishām, from Ziyād ibn ʿAbd Allāh, from Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, who said: ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ told him:

Someone who heard ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit say told me: We were in the mosque reciting, with Abū Bakr among us. We were unlettered, and some of us recited to others. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl came out, followed by a cushion and a fine rug that had been laid out for him. He leaned back and said, “Abū Bakr, will you not tell Muḥammad to bring us a sign, as the earlier messengers brought? Ṣāliḥ brought the she-camel; Moses brought the tablets; David brought the Psalms; Jesus brought the table. And ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy is an eloquent and handsome man.” Abū Bakr wept, and the Messenger of God ﷺ came out. Abū Bakr said, “Come, let us seek the Prophet of God’s help against this hypocrite.” The Messenger of God ﷺ said, “It is not for me to be stood up for; it is only that [people] stand—” to God” . Gabriel came to me and said, “Go out and speak of the blessing God has bestowed upon you and of the favor by which He has distinguished you.” He gave me the good news of ten things that no prophet before me had been granted: God sent me to all people, and commanded me to warn the jinn; God gave me His speech, though I was unlettered, while David had been given the Psalms, Moses the Tablets, and Jesus the Gospel; He forgave me my former and latter sins; God gave me al-Kawthar; God reinforced me with the angels; He granted me victory; He placed terror before me; He made my basin the greatest of basins; He raised my name in the call to prayer ; He will raise me on the Day of Resurrection to a praiseworthy station, while the people hasten toward me with their heads covered; He will raise me on the Day of Resurrection among the first company ; I will enter Paradise with seventy thousand of my community, who will not be called to account; He will raise me on the Day of Resurrection to the highest chamber in the Gardens of Bliss, with none above me except the angels who bear the Throne; He granted me authority and kingship; and He made the spoils lawful for me and my community, though they had not been lawful for anyone before us.

He died at al-Ramla in the year thirty-four. He was known by the kunya Abū al-Walīd.

Qays ibn Saʿd ibn ʿUbāda

They have reports from him, from the Messenger of God ﷺ. Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa and Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maylil, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Umayya, from Qays ibn Saʿd, who said: “I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say, ‘The person riding an animal has the better right to its front seat.’” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related this to us.

The people of Kūfa also share in transmitting this report.

Abū Zurʿa related it to us from Ḥaywa, exactly the same.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from ʿAmr ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbda, from Qays ibn Saʿd, that the Messenger of God ﷺ went out to them one day while they were in the mosque and said: “My Lord has forbidden wine, gambling, the kūba, and the qannīn to me; every intoxicant is forbidden.” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related this to us. Sometimes a phrase is inserted between ʿAmr ibn al-Walīd and Qays: “It reached him.”

Saʿīd ibn ʿUfair related to us; Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb related to us, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Zaḥr, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from Qays ibn Saʿd, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “God has forbidden wine, the kūba, and the qannīn. Beware of al-ghubayrāʾ, for it is a third of the world’s wine.” Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Hubayra, who heard a shaykh relating to Abū Tamīm al-Jayshānī that he had heard Qays ibn Saʿd say from the pulpit: “I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say, ‘Whoever deliberately lies about me, let him take his seat in a house of fire. And whoever drinks wine will come thirsty on the Day of Resurrection. Every intoxicant is forbidden.’” “I heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr say the same thing; they differed only over ‘house’ or ‘bedplace.’” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, and Ṭalq ibn al-Samḥ related this to us.

Qays ibn Saʿd had been appointed governor of Egypt. ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib appointed him over it in the year thirty-seven and dismissed him in the year thirty-eight.

Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Anṣārī

They have reports from him, from the Messenger of God ﷺ. Among them is the report of Bakr ibn Sawāda and Jaʿfar ibn Rabīʿa, from Abū Ḥamza al-Khawlānī, who heard Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh say: “The Messenger of God ﷺ sent out an expedition, and I was among them. He appointed Qays ibn Saʿd ibn ʿUbāda over them. They were hard pressed, so Qays slaughtered nine riding camels for them. They passed by the sea and found that it had cast up a huge sea creature. They stayed there for three days, eating from it, drying strips of its meat, and drawing off its fat into their waterskins. When they came to the Messenger of God ﷺ they told him about Qays. He said, ‘Generosity is a trait of the people of that household.’” They told him about the sea creature, and he said, “Had we known we could reach it and that it would not be gone, I would have wished some of it were with us.”

Shuʿayb ibn Yaḥyā related this to us, from Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb, from Jaʿfar ibn Rabīʿa.

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda. One of the two adds a word or something similar.

Among them is the report of Bakr ibn Muḍar and al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Abū Zurʿa ʿAmr ibn Jābir al-Ḥaḍramī, from Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh, from the Messenger of God ﷺ who said: “Whoever fasts Ramaḍān and follows it with six days of Shawwāl, it is as though he has fasted for a lifetime—or that is the fasting of a lifetime.”

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, and ʿAbd al-Ghaffār ibn Dāwūd related it to us from Bakr ibn Muḍar. He said:

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us from Ibn Lahīʿa and ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ, from al-Layth ibn Saʿd.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū Zurʿa ʿAmr ibn Jābir, from Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh, the Companion of the Prophet ﷺ who heard him say: “The person fleeing from the plague is like the person fleeing from the battle.” ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related this to us.

What indicates that Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh came to Egypt is the report ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yūsuf related to us. Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz al-Tanūkhī related to us, saying: “Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh came to Maslama ibn Mukhallad, who was governor of Egypt. He said to him, ‘Send for ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir al-Juhanī so that I may ask him about a report he heard from the Messenger of God ﷺ.’ He sent for him. He said, ‘I heard—’” It is said that the person who came from Medina to ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir was in fact al-Sāʾib ibn Khallād al-Anṣārī, according to what Yaḥyā ibn Ḥassān mentioned, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, who said: “Al-Sāʾib ibn Khallād al-Anṣārī came to ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir al-Juhanī and said, ‘Did you hear the Messenger of God ﷺ mention anything about concealment ?’ ʿUqba said, ‘I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say, “Whoever conceals a Muslim, God will conceal him.”’” He said, “Did you hear it yourself from the Messenger of God ﷺ?”

He said, “Yes.” He said, “Then he went away; he had come from Medina for no other reason. God knows best.”

He said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related to us; Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb related to us, from ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿAbbās, from Wāhib ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Maʿāfirī, who said: “A man from among the Anṣār, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ came to Maslama ibn Mukhallad and found him asleep. The man said, ‘Wake him.’ They said, ‘Rather, come down and wait until he wakes.’ He said, ‘I will not—’”

“do that.” They woke Maslama, and he came out and said, “Come down.” The man said, “No, not until you send for ʿUqba.” He sent for him, and ʿUqba came. The man said, “Did you hear the Messenger of God ﷺ say, ‘Whoever finds a Muslim exposed and conceals him is as one who has brought a girl buried alive back from her grave’?” ʿUqba said, “I am Abū Ḥammād. I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say that.” Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb did not name the man. God knows best.

Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī

They have reports from him, from the Messenger of God ﷺ; all of them are unusual in their transmission. Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from Sahl ibn Saʿd: A man whose name was Aswad was renamed by the Messenger of God ﷺ “Abyad.” Saʿīd ibn Tulayd related this to us, from Ibn Wahb, from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū Zurʿa ʿAmr ibn Jābir, who said: “I heard Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī say that the Messenger of God ﷺ said, ‘Do not revile Tubbaʿ, for he became a Muslim.’” Abū al-Aswad and ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related this to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Jamīl al-Ḥadhdhāʾ, from Sahl ibn Saʿd, who said: “I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say, ‘O God, may I neither reach a time, nor that time reach me, in which the learned are not followed and the forbearing are not respected. Their hearts are the hearts of non-Arabs, and their tongues are the tongues of Arabs.’” ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related this to us.

Among them is the report of Bakr ibn Muḍar, from ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿUqba, who said that Yaḥyā ibn Maymūn related to him: “I was in the mosque when Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Anṣārī passed by me. He greeted me, then stopped and said, ‘Shall I tell you something I heard from the Messenger of God ﷺ?’ Then I turned to a person beside me and said, ‘There is no one between me and the Messenger of God except this man.’ He said, ‘I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say, “Whoever is in the mosque waiting for the prayer is in prayer.”’” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related this to us. Abū al-Aswad related to us from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yaḥyā— Ibn Maymūn al-Ḥaḍramī said: I heard Sahl ibn Saʿd say: The Messenger of God ﷺ said: “One of you remains in prayer so long as he is in the mosque awaiting the prayer.” Muslima ibn Mukhallad al-Anṣārī They have only one report from him, and no other. It is the report of Mūsā ibn ʿAlī, from his father, who said that he heard him say from the pulpit: “The Messenger of God ﷺ died when I was ten years old.” Only the people of Egypt transmitted from him. The people of Baṣra have one report from him, namely the report of Abū Hilāl al-Rāsibī: Jubla ibn ʿAṭiyya related to us, from Muslima ibn Mukhallad, that he saw Muʿāwiya eating. He said to ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ: “Your cousin is indeed a glutton.” Then he said: “I say this, though I have heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: ‘O God, teach him the Book, establish him firmly in the lands, and protect him from punishment.’” Some traditionists may have inserted a man between Jubla ibn ʿAṭiyya and Muslima. Muslima governed Egypt, and was the first to have Egypt and the Maghrib placed under his authority. He died in the year sixty-two. His kunya was Abū Saʿīd.

Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd al-Anṣārī

They have nearly twenty reports from him. Among them is the report of Ibn Wahb, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿAṭāʾ ibn Dīnār, from Abū Yazīd al-Khawlānī, from Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd, that he heard ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb say:

He heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “The martyrs are four. A man of sound faith meets the enemy and is true to God until he is killed; he is the one toward whom people will raise their eyes like this on the Day of Resurrection.” He raised his head until his cap fell. “I do not know whether it was ʿUmar’s cap or the Messenger of God’s cap. A man of sound faith meets the enemy, and it is as though his skin is being struck with the thorns of the ṭalḥ tree, so great is his fear; a stray arrow strikes him and kills him. He is in the second rank. A man of faith mixes righteous deeds with evil ones, meets the enemy, and is true to God until he is killed; he is in the third rank. A man of faith has been excessive against himself, meets the enemy, and is true to God until he is killed; he is in the fourth rank.” Our father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, who said: Abū Hānīʾ al-Khawlānī related to me, from Abū ʿAlī al-Janbī, from Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “The rider greets the one walking, the one walking greets the one sitting, and the few greet the many.” Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Abū Hānīʾ al-Khawlānī, from ʿAmr ibn Mālik al-Janbī, from Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ said during the Farewell Pilgrimage: “Shall I tell you who the believer is? The one whom people trust with their property and their lives. The Muslim is one from whose tongue and hand people are safe. The striver is one who strives against himself in obedience to God. The emigrant is one who abandons sins and transgressions.” Abū Ṣāliḥ related it to us.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, who said: Abū Shujāʿ Saʿīd ibn Yazīd al-Ḥimyarī related to me, from Khālid ibn Abī ʿImrān, from Ḥanash al-Ṣanʿānī, from Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd, who said: On the day of Khaybar, I bought a necklace containing beads and gold for twelve dinars. I separated its components, and found that the gold was worth more than twelve dinars. I mentioned this to the Messenger of God ﷺ and he said: “It may not be sold until its components have been separated.” Asad ibn Mūsā and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us.

He said: Al-Muqriʾ related to us; Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ related to us; Abū Hānīʾ Ḥumayd ibn Hānīʾ informed me, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, from Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd, who said: A necklace containing gold and beads was brought to the Messenger of God ﷺ to be sold; it was among the spoils. He ordered that the gold in the necklace be removed by itself, then said:

“Gold for gold, weight for weight.” Among them is the report of Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ, who said: Abū Hānīʾ al-Khawlānī related to me that ʿAmr ibn Mālik told him that he heard Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd say: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “Blessed is the one who is guided to Islam, whose livelihood is sufficient, and who is content.” Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū Hānīʾ al-Khawlānī, from ʿAmr ibn Mālik al-Janbī, from Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd, that he heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “I guarantee a house in the outskirts of Paradise for whoever believes in me and submits to Islam. I guarantee a house in the outskirts of Paradise and a house in the middle of Paradise for whoever believes in me, submits to Islam, and emigrates. I guarantee a house in the outskirts of Paradise, a house in the middle of Paradise, a house in the highest part of Paradise, and a house in the highest part of Paradise for whoever believes in me, submits to Islam, emigrates, and strives in the path of God, and who leaves no good to be sought nor evil to be fled; he may die wherever he wishes to die.” Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ, who said: Abū Hānīʾ al-Khawlānī informed me that ʿAmr ibn Mālik al-Janbī told him that he heard Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd relate from the Messenger of God ﷺ that he said: “Whoever dies in one of these ranks will be resurrected in that rank on the Day of Resurrection.” Al-Muqriʾ related it to us from Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ.

Asad ibn Mūsā related it from Ibn al-Mubārak, from Ḥaywa.

Among them is the report of Ḥaywa, from Abū Hānīʾ, that ʿAmr ibn Mālik told him that he heard Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd say: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “The striver is the one who strives against himself.” Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, who said: Abū Marzūq al-Tujībī informed me, from Ḥanash ibn ʿAbd Allāh, from Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ called for a drink. One of us said to him: “Were you not fasting, O Messenger of God?” He replied: “Indeed, but I vomited.” Asad ibn Mūsā, Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār, and ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us.

Among them is the report of Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb and Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū ʿAlī al-Hamdānī, who said: I saw Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd order that the graves of the Muslims in the land of the Romans be leveled with the ground. In his report, Ibn Lahīʿa said: He said, “I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: ‘Level your graves with the ground.’” Al-Muqriʾ related it to us from Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb. He also said: Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū Hānīʾ, from Abū ʿAlī al-Janbī, from Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “There are three about whom you should not ask: a man who leaves the community or disobeys his ruler and dies in disobedience—do not ask about him; a slave woman or male slave who runs away from his master and dies—do not ask about him; and a woman whose husband is away and has provided for her worldly needs, but who, after his departure, makes a display of herself—do not ask about her.” “And there are three about whom you should not ask: a man who contends with God over His mantle [and a man who contends with God over His lower garment]—His mantle is pride and His lower garment is might—and a man who doubts God’s [command].” Among the people of Medina, Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab transmitted from him. Among the people of Syria, Ibn Muḥayriz did so; no one else from the regions transmitted anything from him. He died in the year fifty-three. His kunya was Abū Muḥammad, and Muʿāwiya appointed him judge.

Ruwīfiʿ ibn Thābit al-Anṣārī

They have fewer than ten reports from him. Among them is the report of Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd, who said: Rabīʿa ibn Sulaym, the client of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥassān al-Tujībī, related to me that he heard Ḥanash al-Ṣanʿānī relate that he heard Ruwīfiʿ ibn Thābit say during the expedition of Iyās, before sunset, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said during the expedition of Khaybar: “I have been told that you are exchanging a measure of gold for half or two-thirds of a measure. That is not valid: it must be a measure for a measure, and weight for weight.” The Messenger of God ﷺ also said: “Whoever believes in God and the Last Day must not use an animal from the spoils, then, after wearing it down, return it to the spoils; nor a garment he wears, then, after wearing it out, return it to the spoils.” The Messenger of God ﷺ also said: “Whoever believes in God and the Last Day must not let his water irrigate another man’s child.” Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam related it to us.

Among them is the report of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAyyāsh al-Qitbānī, from his father, from Shayīm ibn Baytān, from Shaybān Ibn Umayyah, from Ruwayfiʿ ibn Thābit, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Whoever is turned away from something by an omen has committed an act of associating others with God.” Idrīs ibn Yaḥyā al-Khawlānī related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn ʿAyyāsh, from his father, from Shayīm ibn Baytān, from Shaybān ibn Umayyah, from Ruwayfiʿ ibn Thābit, who said: I was at a gathering in which the Messenger of God ﷺ was present. I was the youngest of them. The Messenger of God looked at me and said: “Ruwayfiʿ, perhaps you will live a long time. Tell the people that whoever cleans himself after relieving himself with the dung of an animal or with a bone, or hangs a bowstring intending it as an amulet, or knots his beard during prayer, has no covenant of protection from Muḥammad.” Idrīs ibn Yaḥyā related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿah, from Bakr ibn Sawādah, from Ziyād ibn Nuʿaym, from Wafāʾ ibn Shurayḥ al-Ḥaḍramī, from Ruwayfiʿ ibn Thābit, from the Messenger of God ﷺ, who said: “Whoever invokes blessings upon Muḥammad and says, ‘O God, grant him the station nearest to You on the Day of Resurrection,’ will be entitled to my intercession.”

Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam, Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār, and Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us.

Some of them say: “And place him in the station nearest to You.”

Among them is the report of al-Mufaḍḍal ibn Faḍālah, from ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿAbbās al-Qitbānī, from Shayīm ibn Baytān, who heard Shaybān ibn Umayyah al-Qitbānī, from Ruwayfiʿ ibn Thābit, who said: In the time of the Messenger of God ﷺ, one of us would take his brother’s worn-out mount on the understanding that he would give him half of what he captured. Sometimes the arrowhead and feathers would fall to one of us, while the other got the shaft.

Ruwayfiʿ said: The Messenger of God said to me, “Ruwayfiʿ, perhaps you will live long after me. Tell the people that whoever knots his beard, wears a bowstring around his neck, or cleans himself after relieving himself with animal dung or with a bone—Muḥammad is free of him.”

ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿAbbās informed me, from Shayīm ibn Baytān, from Abū Sālim al-Jayshānī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, that he heard him mention this report while he was stationed at the fortress of Bāb al-Yūn.

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: Abū al-Aswad used to pronounce it with a mīm, and would say: “It was so named because they used to say, ‘Who is fighting today?’”

Abū Hurayrah

They have nearly twenty reports from him. Among them12 is the report of Ibn Lahīʿah, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, who was informed by Thābit ibn al-Ḥārith that he heard Abū Hurayrah report from the Messenger of God ﷺ, who said: “Faith is Yemeni, understanding of religion is Yemeni, and wisdom is Yemeni. The people of Yemen have come to you: they are tenderer in feeling and softer of heart. Unbelief is toward the east; pride and conceit are among the horsemen and the loud-voiced camel herders, the people of wool tents; and tranquility is among the sheep herders.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among them is the report of Mūsā ibn ʿAlī, from his father, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān, from Abū Hurayrah, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “The worst things in a man are unnerving avarice and overwhelming cowardice.” Al-Muqriʾ and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿah, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Luhayʿah ibn ʿUqbah, from Abū al-Ward, from Abū Hurayrah, who said: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “Beware of horses granted as extra booty: if they meet the enemy, they flee; and if booty is won, they embezzle it.” Aḥmad ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Sarḥ related it to us, from Ibn Wahb.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿah, from Darrāj Abū al-Samḥ, from Ibn Ḥujayrah, from Abū Hurayrah, from the Messenger of God ﷺ, who said: “Men whom neither trade nor sale distracts from the remembrance of God.” He said: “They are those who travel through the land, seeking the bounty of God.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿah, from Darrāj, from Ibn Ḥujayrah, from Abū Hurayrah, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “By Him in whose hand is my soul, everything will contend on the Day of Resurrection—even two ewes will contend over what they butted one another.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿah, from Darrāj, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥujayrah, who said: I heard Abū Hurayrah say: The Messenger of God ﷺ said: “The likeness of one who learns but neither teaches nor speaks of what he has learned is that of one who hoards a treasure and spends none of it.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among them is the report of Salāmān ibn ʿĀmir al-Shaʿbānī, who said: Abū ʿUthmān al-Aṣbaḥī told me, from Abū Hurayrah, that he said: The Messenger of God ﷺ said: “If you knew what I know, you would laugh little and weep much.” They said: “What is that, Messenger of God?” He said: “Time will draw close; hypocrisy will appear; mercy will be withdrawn; trustworthiness will be taken away; the trustworthy will be suspected, and the suspect will be trusted. The dark eminences have encamped among you.”

Abū Hurayrah said: “I heard it from no one before the Messenger of God.” They said: “Messenger of God, what are the dark eminences?” He said: “Tribulations, like patches of a dark night.” Al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and Ṭalq ibn al-Samḥ related it to us.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Darrāj Abū al-Samḥ, from Ibn Ḥujayrah, from Abū Hurayrah, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “When one of you prays, let him not spread his hands on the ground as a dog spreads them; let him keep his thighs together.”

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: Al-Layth transmitted no report from Darrāj except this one.

He said: Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related to us: Ibn Lahīʿah related to us, from Suwayd al-Ḥāsib, that he saw Abū Hurayrah praying in the mosque of Miṣr.

He said: Ḥabīb ibn Marzūq, Mālik’s scribe, related to us: The son of the brother of— Ibn Shihāb, from Ibn Shihāb, from al-Qāsim ibn Muḥammad, said: Abū Hurayrah’s name was ʿAbd Shams; it is also said that it was ʿAbd Nahm. God knows best. He died in Medina in the year fifty-nine; it is also said, fifty-eight.

Abū Baṣrah al-Ghifārī, whose name was Ḥumayl ibn Baṣrah

They have five reports from him. Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Khālid ibn Yazīd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, from Abū Baṣrah, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “We are setting out tomorrow, God willing, to the Jews. When they greet you, say, ‘Upon you.’” ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us.

ʿAlī ibn Maʿbad related to us: ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAmr al-Jazarī related to us, from Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr Marthad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Yazanī, from Abū Baṣrah, from the Messenger of God ﷺ, the like of it.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Khayr ibn Nuʿaym, from Ibn Hubayrah, from Abū Tamīm, from Abū Baṣrah, that the Messenger of God ﷺ led them one day in the afternoon prayer at al-Mukhammaṣ—one of their valleys. Then he turned away and said: “This prayer was presented to those who came before you, but they were remiss concerning it and abandoned it. Whoever among you prays it, God will multiply his reward twofold. There is no prayer after it until the witness rises.”

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us, from al-Layth. He said: My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related to us, from Ibn Lahīʿah and Idrīs ibn Yaḥyā, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAyyāsh al-Qitbānī, from Ibn Hubayrah, from Abū Tamīm, from Abū Baṣrah, from the Messenger of God ﷺ, a report similar to it.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Kulayb ibn Dhuʾl al-Ḥaḍramī, from ʿUbayd ibn Jabr, who said: He traveled with Abū Baṣrah al-Ghifārī during Ramaḍān. When they set out from al-Fusṭāṭ, he asked for food while we could see al-Fusṭāṭ. He called for the dining cloth, and I said: “Shall we eat, when we can still see al-Fusṭāṭ if we wish?” He said: “Would we turn away from the practice of the Messenger of God and his Companions?” So we broke our fast.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us, and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us, from Ibn Lahīʿah.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Mūsā ibn Wardān, from Abū al-Haytham: he asked Abū Baṣra about the conversion of Ghifār. He said:

“A year of hardship and little rain befell us. We talked of going to the Messenger of God ﷺ to obtain some food with him and return to our mountain. So we set out for the Messenger of God, not wishing to embrace Islam. He said, ‘Who are these people?’ We said, ‘A group of Banū Ghifār.’ He said, ‘Are they Muslims or waṣābī?’13

“We said, ‘Rather, waṣābī.’14 We stayed that day. When night came, the Messenger of God ﷺ said to his Companions:

“‘Let each one of you take one of them by the hand.’ God enabled me to take the Messenger of God by the hand. He took me to his house. He had eight she-goats that he milked. He called each she-goat by its name. He called Mawhiba for one of them, and she brought it. He milked it and gave me its milk to drink. It was as though I had drunk nothing. Then he called for another, and he kept on until he had given me the milk of seven she-goats. I left the eighth only out of self-restraint. Mawhiba grew angry with an anger such as I had never seen, and loathed me with a loathing such as I had never seen, though she did not show it to me in the presence of the Messenger of God ﷺ.

“Then the Messenger of God ﷺ called her and said, ‘Mawhiba, put this man up tonight in a room, and do not bolt the door against him, for he has had enough to eat.’ The servant girl took me and put me in the room, then shut the door on me angrily. My bowels troubled me throughout that night, until morning, and I had soiled all my clothes. The Messenger of God ﷺ called for water and washed me, then wrapped me in a shawl of his own. In the morning he took me to the mosque, where I found my companions gathered in a circle; they had embraced Islam, and I embraced Islam.

“When night came, the Messenger of God ﷺ instructed his Companions that each man should take his companion by the hand and put him up for the night. The Messenger of God ﷺ took me by the hand, and I went with him to his house. He called Mawhiba and said, ‘Bring me so-and-so.’ He milked her, and I drank less than half her milk. The Messenger of God ﷺ said, ‘Abū Baṣra, the unbeliever eats with seven intestines, and the believer eats with one intestine.’”

Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Hubayra, that Abū Tamīm al-Jayshānī informed him that he had heard ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ say: “A man of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ informed me that he had heard the Prophet ﷺ say: ‘God has granted you an additional prayer, so perform it between the evening prayer and the dawn prayer: the witr, the witr.’”

“Indeed, it was Abū Baṣra al-Ghifārī.” Abū Tamīm said: “Abū Dharr and I were sitting together when Abū Dharr took me by the hand. We went to Abū Baṣra and found him at the door that led to the residence of ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ. Abū Dharr said, ‘Abū Baṣra, did you hear the Messenger of God ﷺ say, “God has granted you an additional prayer, so perform it between the evening prayer and dawn: the witr, the witr”?’ He said, ‘Yes.’ He said, ‘You heard him?’ He said, ‘Yes.’ He said, ‘You heard him?’ He said, ‘Yes.’”

Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr related it to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa and ʿAmr ibn Sawwād, from Ibn Wahb, from Ibn Lahīʿa. None other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

Abū Dharr al-Ghifārī

They have reports from him. Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb: Abū Sālim al-Jayshānī came to Abū Umayya at his home and said, “I heard Abū Dharr say, ‘I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “If one of you loves his companion, let him visit him at his home and tell him that he loves him.”’” “So I have come to you at your home.” Abū al-Aswad related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn ʿAmr al-Maʿāfirī, who heard Yazīd ibn Tuʿaym al-Tujībī say: “I heard Abū Dharr al-Ghifārī, seated by the pulpit in the mosque of Fusṭāṭ, say:

“‘I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “Whoever draws near to God by a span, God draws near to him by an arm’s length; and whoever draws near to God by an arm’s length, God draws near to him by a fathom. God is higher and mightier.”’” He said it three times. Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Darrāj, from Abū al-Maythāʾ, from Abū Dharr, who said: “For six days the Messenger of God ﷺ said to me, ‘Understand what I am telling you.’ Then, on the seventh day, he said, ‘I counsel you to fear God in what you do in private and in public. When you do wrong, follow it with a good deed. Do not ask anyone for anything, even if your whip falls. Do not take charge of a trust, do not take charge of an orphan, and do not judge between two people.’”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār, Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr, and ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us. Abū al-Aswad did not mention Abū al-Maythāʾ.

Among them is the report of Rushdayn ibn Saʿd and Ibn Wahb, from Ḥarmala ibn ʿImrān al-Tujībī, from Ibn Shumāsa al-Mahrī, who said: “I heard Abū Dharr say that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: ‘You will conquer a land where the qīrāṭ is mentioned, so treat its people well, for they have a covenant of protection and kinship. When you see two men fighting over a space the size of a brick, leave that place.’” He passed by ʿAbd al-Raḥmān and Rabīʿa, the sons of Sharāḥbīl ibn Ḥasana, as they were disputing over a space the size of a brick, and he left that place.

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related it to us, from Rushdayn ibn Saʿd. And ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it from Ibn Wahb, from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Among them is the report of Ibn Wahb, from ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, that Abū Sālim al-Jayshānī related to him, from Abū Dharr, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said to him: “‘What do you think of Juʿayl?’ I said, ‘A poor man, like an ordinary person.’ He said, ‘And what do you think of so-and-so?’ I said, ‘A leader among the people.’ He said, ‘Juʿayl is better than an earthful—or a thousand, or something like that—of so-and-so.’ I said, ‘Messenger of God, so-and-so is as you say, yet you treat him as you do.’ He said, ‘He is the chief of his people, and I am winning them over by means of him.’”

He said: Saʿīd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Tulayd related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Hubayra, from Abū Tamīm al-Jayshānī, that Abū Dharr related to him: “I was with the Messenger of God ﷺ until he entered his house. He kept saying, ‘Other than the Antichrist, I fear for my community; other than the Antichrist, I fear for my community.’ When I feared he would enter his house without explaining what he meant, I said, ‘What is it besides the Antichrist that you fear for your community, Messenger of God?’ He said, ‘Misleading—or astray—leaders.’”

Ṭalq ibn al-Samḥ, Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr, and Hāniʾ ibn al-Mutawakkil related it to us.

Among them is the report of Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Jaʿfar, from Sālim ibn Abī Sālim al-Jayshānī, from his father, from Abū Dharr, who said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ said: ‘I see that you are weak, and I desire for you what I desire for myself. Do not take authority over two people, and do not take charge of an orphan’s property.’”

Al-Muqriʾ related it to us, from Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū Qubayl, who said: “I heard Mālik ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bardādī—”

“—relate from Abū Dharr that he said: ‘I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “I would not wish to have this mountain of gold, spend it, and have it accepted from me, while leaving behind nine uqiyyas of it.”’” “I adjure you by God, ʿUthmān: did you hear it from the Messenger of God ﷺ three times?” He said, “Yes.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Bakr ibn ʿAmr, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd al-Ḥaḍramī, from Ibn Ḥujayra the Elder, from Abū Dharr, who said: “I said, ‘Messenger of God, will you not appoint me to a position?’ He struck my shoulder with his hand and then said, ‘Abū Dharr, you are weak. Authority is a trust, and on the Day of Resurrection it will bring disgrace and regret, except for the one who takes it rightfully and fulfills what is incumbent upon him in it.’”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us. He said: Ibn Lahīʿa related to us, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, who said: “I heard Ibn Ḥujayra the Elder say, ‘Someone who heard Abū Dharr related to me.’”15

He died at al-Rabadha in the year thirty-two. Ibn Masʿūd performed the funeral prayer over him as he was returning from Medina to Kūfa. His name was Jundub ibn Junāda, though it is said that it was Burayr, according to what ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Hishām related to us.

Hubayb ibn Mughaffal al-Ghifārī, companion of the Wādī Hubayb

They have one report from him from the Prophet ﷺ. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, that Aslam Abū ʿImrān related to him: “Muslama ibn Mukhallad sent me to the ruler of Abyssinia. When I arrived, there were people with him waiting for permission to enter, among them Hubayb ibn Mughaffal al-Ghifārī, a Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ and Muḥammad ibn ʿAlba al-Qurashī. He gave Muḥammad ibn ʿAlba permission to enter. The man stood up, dragging his lower garment. Hubayb looked at him and said, ‘I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “Whoever drags his lower garment in arrogance, the Fire will trample him.”’”

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us. Ibn Wahb also transmitted it, from Qurra ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from Ibn Abī Ḥabīb, that Abū ʿImrān informed him, from Hubayb ibn Mughaffal, that he had heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say the like of it.

They have no report from him from the Prophet ﷺ—other than this one.

They have accounts from him concerning himself. Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, who heard Abū Tamīm al-Jayshānī say: “We went on a campaign with ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, the campaign of Ṭarābulus. We gathered in an assembly, and with us was Hubayb ibn Mughaffal. We spoke about making up the fasts owed from Ramaḍān. Hubayb said: ‘The fasts owed from Ramaḍān are not to be divided up.’ ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ said: ‘There is no harm in dividing up the fasts owed from Ramaḍān, provided you keep count of the number; it is only a number.’” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār narrated it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Usāma ibn Isāf al-Ghifārī, who said: Abū Ṣāliḥ al-Ghifārī told us: “I went out with Hubayb ibn Mughaffal al-Ghifārī, the Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ—as he was heading to his family. He had been told that one of his sons was ill. When noon came, he continued on as he was. I said, ‘The prayer, may God set you right.’ He continued on as he was until the afternoon came. Then he dismounted and joined the noon and afternoon prayers together.”

No one narrated from him except the people of Egypt.

ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir al-Juhanī

They have from him nearly a hundred reports from the Messenger of God. Among them is the report of Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ, from Bakr ibn ʿAmr al-Maʿāfirī, from Mishraḥ ibn Hāʿān, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, that the Messenger of God said:

“Wickedness is seventy parts: sixty-nine parts belong to the Berbers, and one part to jinn and humankind.” Abū Zurʿa Wahb Allāh ibn Rāshid narrated it to us.

Among them is the report of Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb, who said: Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb told me: “I heard Abū al-Khayr Marthad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Yazanī say: ‘I saw Abū Tamīm al-Jayshānī ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik perform two rakʿas when he heard the call to the sunset prayer. I went to ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir al-Juhanī and said, “Shall I tell you something astonishing about Abū Tamīm? He performs two rakʿas before the sunset prayer.” I meant by this to find fault with him. ʿUqba said, “Indeed, we used to do that in the time of the Messenger of God.” I said, “What prevents you now?” He replied, “Preoccupation.”’” Al-Muqriʾ narrated it to us from Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, that the Messenger of God gave him sheep to distribute among his Companions as sacrificial animals. One young male goat remained, so he mentioned it to the Messenger of God, who said: “Sacrifice it yourself.” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, narrated it to us. Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, and Asad ibn Mūsā also narrated it to us.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, who said: “We said, ‘Messenger of God, you send us out, and we stay with a people who do not receive us hospitably. What do you advise concerning that?’ The Messenger of God said to us: ‘If you stay with a people and they provide you with what is due to a guest, accept it. If they do not, then take from them the guest’s due that they ought to provide.’” Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, and Asad ibn Mūsā narrated it to us. Asad did not mention “you send us out.”

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, who said: “A silk furūj was presented to the Messenger of God. He put it on, then prayed in it. Afterward he took it off forcefully, as though disliking it, and then said: ‘This is not fitting for the God-fearing.’”16

Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, and Asad ibn Mūsā narrated it to us; Asad did not mention “as though disliking it.”

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Kaʿb ibn ʿAlqama, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Shimāsa, from Abū al-Khayr, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, that the Messenger of God said: “The expiation for a vow is the expiation for an oath.” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār narrated it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Mishraḥ ibn Hāʿān, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, that the Messenger of God said: “What an excellent household: Abū ʿAbd Allāh, Umm ʿAbd Allāh, and ʿAbd Allāh.” Al-Muqriʾ narrated it to us.

Among them is the report of Ḥaywa and Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn ʿAmr al-ʿĀfirī, from Mishraḥ ibn Hāʿān, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, that the Messenger of God said: “If there were to be a prophet after me, it would be ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.” Al-Muqriʾ narrated it to us from Ḥaywa. ʿAbd al-Ghaffār ibn Dāwūd al-Ḥarrānī narrated it from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Mishraḥ, who said: “I heard ʿUqba say that the Messenger of God said:

‘If the Qurʾān were placed in a hide and then cast into the fire, it would not burn.’” He said: Al-Muqriʾ, Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr, and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār narrated it to us.

A report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Mishraḥ ibn Hāʿān, who said: “I heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say:

‘I heard the Messenger of God say: “Every dead person’s deeds are sealed, except for the one stationed on the frontier in the path of God: his reward for his deeds continues to accrue until he is resurrected.”’” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, al-Muqriʾ, and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār narrated it to us. Abū al-Aswad said: “His deeds continue to accrue to him until he is resurrected, and he is protected from the interrogator of the grave.” Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, who said: “I heard Mishraḥ ibn Hāʿān say: ‘I heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say: “I asked the Messenger of God, saying, ‘Messenger of God, has Sūrat al-Ḥajj been given precedence over the Qurʾān because it contains two prostrations?’ The Messenger of God said, ‘Yes; and whoever does not prostrate at them should not recite it.’”’” My father, Abū al-Aswad, and Asad ibn Mūsā narrated it to us. In his version, Abū al-Aswad said: “I said, ‘Messenger of God, there are two prostrations in Sūrat al-Ḥajj.’”

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Mishraḥ ibn Hāʿān and Ḥaywa, from Khālid ibn ʿUbayd, from Mishraḥ, that he heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say that he heard the Messenger of God say: “Whoever hangs an amulet, may God not fulfill his purpose; and whoever hangs a cowrie shell, may God not grant him repose.” Abū al-Aswad narrated it to us from Ibn Lahīʿa and al-Muqriʾ; and Abū Zurʿa Wahb ibn Rāshid narrated it from Ḥaywa. Al-Muqriʾ said: “Whoever attaches an amulet.”

Among them is the report of Ḥarmala ibn ʿImrān, who said: “I heard Abū ʿAshāna say: ‘I heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say: “I heard the Messenger of God say: ‘Whoever has three daughters and is patient with them, feeds them, gives them drink, and clothes them from his own means, they will be a shield for him from the Fire.’”’” He said: Al-Muqriʾ and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ narrated it to us.

Among them is the report of Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb, from ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith, that Abū ʿAshāna told him, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, that the Messenger of God said: “Whoever performs ablution, gathers his clothes around himself, and then goes out to the mosque, his two recording angels write ten good deeds for him for every step. He remains in prayer for as long as he waits for the prayer.

He is recorded among those praying from the time he leaves his home until he returns to it.” Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam narrated it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Maʿrūf ibn Suwayd al-Judhāmī, from Abū ʿAshāna, who heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say: “One day I was with the Messenger of God, and he said: ‘Whoever here is of Maʿadd, let him stand.’ I stood up. He said, ‘Sit down.’ He said this three times; each time I stood up, he said, ‘Sit down.’ I said, ‘Then from whom are we, Messenger of God?’ He said: ‘You are of Quḍāʿa ibn Mālik ibn Ḥimyar.’” ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama narrated it to us. Saʿīd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Talīd narrated it to us from Ibn Wahb, from Maʿrūf. ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ narrated it to us from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Mishraḥ, from ʿUqba; no one transmits it from Mishraḥ from ʿUqba except ʿUthmān.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū ʿAshāna, from ʿUqba, that he heard him say, I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ—say: “Whoever says of me what I did not say, let him prepare a house for himself in Hell.”

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū ʿAshāna, who heard ʿUqba report that the Messenger of God ﷺ—used to forbid his family jewelry and silk, saying: “If you love the adornment and silk of Paradise, do not wear them in this world.”

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama transmitted it to us.

Among them is the report of Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb, who said: Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz and Abū Marḥūm related to me, from Yazīd ibn Muḥammad al-Qurashī, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—commanded me to recite the two protective sūras after every prayer.

Al-Muqriʾ transmitted it to us, from Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ transmitted it to us, from al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Ḥunayn ibn Abī Ḥakīm, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir.

Among them is the report of Mūsā ibn ʿAlī, from his father, who heard him say: I heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say: The Messenger of God ﷺ—used to forbid us to pray or bury our dead at three times: when the sun rises until it has risen high; when the sun reaches its zenith until it declines; and when the sun approaches setting until it sets.

Al-Muqriʾ and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ transmitted it to us.

Among them is the report of Mūsā ibn ʿAlī, from his father, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, that the Messenger of God ﷺ—said:

“The Day of Sacrifice, the Day of ʿArafa, and the Days of Tashrīq are our festival, we Muslims; they are days of eating and drinking.”

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ transmitted it to us.

Among them is the report of Qubāth ibn Razīn, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, who said: I heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say: We were in the mosque learning the Qurʾān. The Messenger of God ﷺ—came in to us and greeted us; we returned his greeting. He said: “Learn the Qurʾān and retain it”—and I think he said, “and recite it melodiously. By Him in whose hand is my soul, it slips away faster than she-camels from their tethers.” He said: Al-Muqriʾ transmitted it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, that the Messenger of God ﷺ—said of a man called Dhū al-Bijādayn: “He is one who is deeply devoted,” because he often remembered God through the Qurʾān and supplication, and raised his voice. He said: Asad ibn Mūsā transmitted it to us. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: No one transmitted this report except Asad ibn Mūsā.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from Rabīʿa ibn Qays al-Junbī, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, who said: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ—say: “Whoever performs ablution and performs it well, then prays a prayer without being heedless or distracted, will have the sins he committed before it expiated.” ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said:

I do not recall who transmitted it to us from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Ibn Shumāsa, who heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say: One day we prayed with the Messenger of God ﷺ—and he prolonged the standing for us. When the Messenger of God prayed, he would make it brief. During that standing of his, we could hear nothing from him, except that he said: “My Lord, and they are among them.” Then we saw him reach out with his hand as if to take something. The Messenger of God then bowed, and after that he hastened. When he had given the salutation, he sat down, and we sat around him. He said: “I know that the length of my standing troubled you.” We said:

“Yes, Messenger of God. We heard you say, ‘My Lord, and they are among them.’” He said: “By Him in whose hand is my soul, nothing of what you have been promised in the Hereafter was hidden from me in this place of mine. The Fire was even shown to me. When something from it came toward me and drew level with my shoulder, I feared it would engulf you, so I said, ‘O my Lord, and they are among them.’ God turned it away from you, and it receded in fragments, like rugs. I looked into it, and there I saw ʿImrān ibn Ḥarthān—or Jarban; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān was uncertain—the brother of Banū Ghifār, reclining in Hell on his bow. And there I saw the woman with the cat: she had tied it up, neither feeding it nor letting it loose to seek food, until it died in that state.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār transmitted it to us.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Ibn Shumāsa, who heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say: The Messenger of God ﷺ—said: “A believer is a believer’s brother. It is not lawful for a believer to outbid his brother in a sale until he relinquishes it, or to propose marriage to a woman to whom his brother has proposed until he relinquishes it.” ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ transmitted it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Wāhib ibn ʿAbd Allāh, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Shumāsa, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, that the Messenger of God ﷺ—said: “One who dies of pleurisy is a martyr.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama transmitted it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Razīq al-Thaqafī, who heard him say: I heard Ibn Shumāsa relate from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir that the Messenger of God ﷺ—said: “Whoever does not accept God’s dispensation will bear a sin as great as the mountains of ʿArafāt.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār transmitted it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yaʿqūb, from Ibn Shumāsa al-Mahrī, who said to ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir: “You keep going back and forth between these two targets, though you are an old man and that is difficult for you.” ʿUqba said: “Were it not for words I heard from the Messenger of God ﷺ—I would not burden myself with it.” Al-Ḥārith said: I asked Ibn Shumāsa, “What were they?” He said: “He said: ‘Whoever learns archery and then abandons it is not one of us—or has disobeyed.’”

Al-Ḥārith said: I think he said it this way.

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama transmitted it to us. In ʿAbd al-Malik’s report, Faqīm al-Lakhmī said to ʿUqba: “You keep going back and forth between these two targets.”

Among them is the report of Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ and Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd, from Bakr ibn ʿAmr, who said: I heard Shuʿayb ibn Zurʿa, who heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say that he heard the Messenger of God ﷺ—say to his Companions:

“Do not frighten yourselves after you have been secure.” They said: “Messenger of God, what would frighten us?” He said: “Debt.”

Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam transmitted it to us, from Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd; and al-Muqriʾ, from Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Hubayra and al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Jubayr, who heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say: The Messenger of God ﷺ—“forbade cauterization and drinking hot water. When he applied kohl, he did so an odd number of times, and when he performed istijmār, he did so an odd number of times.”

Asad ibn Mūsā and ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ transmitted it to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Hubayra; and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū Qubayl, who said: I heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say:

I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ—say: “The ruin of my community lies in the Book and milk.” They said: “Messenger of God, what are the Book and milk?” He said: “They learn the Book and interpret it in a way other than that in which God sent it down; and they love milk, so they forsake the congregational prayers and the Friday prayers.”

Abū Qubayl said: I heard nothing from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir besides this. Al-Muqriʾ and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār transmitted it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Isḥāq, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Tujībī, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, who said: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ—say: “The collector of unlawful tolls will not enter Paradise.”

It was transmitted to us by ʿAlī ibn Maʿbad, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAmr al-Jazarī.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, that Hishām ibn Abī Ruqayya told him he had heard Maslama ibn Mukhallad say: “What would lead a Muslim man to wear silk, when he has enough in coarse cloth and linen? Here among you is one who can tell you about the Messenger of God—so stand, ʿUqba.” ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir stood and said: “I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: ‘Whoever deliberately attributes a lie to me, let him take his seat in the Fire.’” “And I heard him say: ‘Whoever wears silk in this world, God will forbid it to him in the Hereafter.’” He said: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama transmitted it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿUqba ibn Muslim, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “When you see God giving people what they ask for while they persist in disobeying Him, that is only a gradual drawing of them on by Him.” Then he recited: “When they forgot what they had been reminded of …” to the end of the verse. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbād al-ʿAbdī transmitted it to us.

Among these is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Aslam Abū ʿImrān, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir, who said: “I followed the Messenger of God ﷺ while he was riding. I placed my hand on his foot and said: ‘Recite to me from Sūrat Hūd or Sūrat Yūsuf.’ He said: ‘Nothing you recite is more eloquent before God than “Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of daybreak.”’”

Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, and Asad ibn Mūsā transmitted it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from Abū Saʿīd al-Qitbānī, from Abū Tamīm al-Jayshānī, from ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir: his sister had vowed to make the pilgrimage on foot, without a head covering. This reached the Prophet ﷺ and he said: “Let her make the pilgrimage riding, with her head covered, and let her fast.”

Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār transmitted it to us. Abū al-Aswad said, on the authority of Bakr, that he heard it from ʿUqba, but he did not include “with her head covered.”

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn ʿAmr al-Maʿāfirī, from someone who heard ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir say: “The Messenger of God ﷺ sent me as a collector. I asked his permission for us to eat from the alms, and he gave us permission.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār transmitted it to us.

Among these is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, that Ibn Shumāsa told him that ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir stood during a prayer in which he was to sit. The people said, “Glory be to God! Glory be to God!” and he understood what they meant. When he had completed his prayer, he performed two prostrations while seated and said: “I heard what you said. This is the practice.”

Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ transmitted it to us. My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, also transmitted it to us: Bakr ibn Muḍar told us, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Ibn Shumāsa, from ʿUqba, with a similar report.

He said: Among those who shared in transmitting from him were, in Medina, Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab and Muʿādh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥabīb; in Kūfa, Qays ibn Abī Ḥāzim; and in Baṣra, al-Ḥasan ibn Abī al-Ḥasan—but this is not sound. He was the city’s jurisconsult, and he died in Egypt during the caliphate of Muʿāwiya. His teknonym was Abū Ḥammād.

Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Juhanī

They have two reports from him. One is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Abū al-Khayr, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Juhanī: The Messenger of God ﷺ sold a man for a debt, in what is called “sarraq”—ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: “That is how I found it in my book.” I discussed it with some of our companions, and one of them said: “It is only Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥublī, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Qaynī, who was one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ.” He said: “A man who had recited Sūrat al-Baqara arrived with a garment, and sold it for ‘sarraq.’ They disputed over it, and he disappeared from him. Then he caught up with him, brought him to the Prophet ﷺ and the Messenger of God said to him: ‘Sell “sarraq.”’ He went away, and a man among the Companions of the Messenger of God bargained for it for three days. Then he changed his mind and set him free. God knows best.”17

The other is the report of Ibn Isḥāq, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Juhanī: The Messenger of God ﷺ saw two riders and said: “Two men of Kinda, or two men of Madhḥij.” When they came to him, they were two men of Madhḥij. One of them said: “Messenger of God, what is the reward for one who saw you, believed in you, and affirmed you?” He said: “Blessedness.” He passed his hand over the man’s hand, then the man went away. The other did the same.

No one other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him. Ibn Isḥāq also transmitted, through this chain, from— Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Tomorrow we are riding out to the Jews.” ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: “That is an error. It was Abū Baṣra.” In this, al-Layth and Ibn Lahīʿa contradicted Ibn Isḥāq, and they know better about it.

Muʿādh ibn Anas al-Juhanī

They have nearly forty reports from him. Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid al-Ḥimyarī, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh ibn Anas al-Juhanī, from his father Muʿādh, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said:

“Whoever recites ‘Say: He is God, the One’ ten times, completing it, God will build for him a house in Paradise.” ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said: “Then we shall do it often, Messenger of God!” He said: “God is more abundant and more excellent.”

He said: Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār transmitted it to us.

Among these is the report of Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd, who said: Abū Marḥūm told me, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh al-Juhanī, from his father, that a man came to a gathering in which the Messenger of God ﷺ was present, and said: “Peace be upon you.” He returned the greeting and said: “Ten good deeds.” Then another came and said: “Peace be upon you and God’s mercy.” He said: “Twenty.” Then another came and said: “Peace be upon you and God’s mercy and blessings.” He said: “Thirty.” Then another came and said: “Peace be upon you and God’s mercy, blessings, and forgiveness.” He said: “Forty.” And he said: “This is how merits are attained.” Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam transmitted it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Among the best of merits is that you maintain ties with one who severs them from you, give to one who withholds from you, and pardon one who wrongs you.” Abū al-Aswad transmitted it to us. Among these is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb and Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh ibn Anas, from his father—who was one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ—who said: “Ride these animals while they are sound, and leave them sound; do not take them as chairs.” Al-Layth said: Sahl ibn Muʿādh himself told me this report from his father, from the Messenger of God ﷺ. Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ transmitted it to us.

Among these is the report of Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb, Ibn Lahīʿa, and Rashdīn ibn Saʿd, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Whoever keeps watch at night in God’s path, volunteering to guard a vulnerable point of the Muslims—the ruler will not seize him—will not see the Fire with his own eyes, except as much as is needed to fulfill the oath.” For God ﵎ has said: “There is none of you who will not come to it.” Muḥammad ibn al-Mutawakkil transmitted it to us, from Rashdīn ibn Saʿd; and Abū al-Aswad, from Ibn Lahīʿa; and my father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, from Ibn Wahb, from Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb.

Among these is the report of Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Whoever remains at his place of prayer after finishing the dawn prayer until he performs two units of the forenoon prayer, saying nothing but good, will have his sins forgiven, even if they are like the foam of the sea.” Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr transmitted it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Whoever is fasting, visits a sick person, and attends a funeral will be forgiven, unless he commits something afterward.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār transmitted it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa and Rashdīn ibn Saʿd, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “The one who laughs during prayer, the one who looks around, and the one who cracks his fingers are all alike.” He said: Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam transmitted it to us, from Rashdīn ibn Saʿd; and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār, from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Among these is the report of Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb, from Abū Marḥūm ʿAbd al-Raḥīm ibn Maymūn, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh and Rashdīn ibn Saʿd, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, that the Messenger of God ﷺ “forbade sitting with one’s legs drawn up on Friday while the imam is preaching.” Muḥammad ibn Yaḥyā transmitted it to us, from al-Muqriʾ; and Ḥajjāj ibn Rashdīn, from his father.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh ibn Anas, from his father.

Muʿādh ibn Jabal asked the Messenger of God about the best form of faith. He replied: “To love for God’s sake, to hate for God’s sake, and to employ your tongue in remembrance of God.” He asked, “And what else, Messenger of God?” He replied: “To wish for people what you wish for yourself, to dislike for them what you dislike for yourself, and to speak good or remain silent.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among them is the report of Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb, from Abū Marḥūm ʿAbd al-Raḥīm ibn Maymūn, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh ibn Anas, from his father, that the Messenger of God said: “Whoever eats food and says, ‘Praise be to God, who fed me this and provided it for me, without any power or strength on my part,’ will be forgiven his previous sins. And whoever puts on a garment and says, ‘Praise be to God, who clothed me with this and provided it for me, without any power or strength on my part,’ will be forgiven his previous sins.”

Muḥammad ibn Yaḥyā related it to us, from al-Muqriʾ.
