Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh ibn Anas, from his father, from the Messenger of God, who said: “God has servants with whom He will not speak on the Day of Resurrection, whom He will not purify, and upon whom He will not look.” They asked, “Who are they, Messenger of God?” He replied: “One who disowns his parents out of aversion to them; one who disowns his child; and a man whom people have treated generously, but who disavows their kindness and disowns them.”

Abū al-Aswad related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, that the Messenger of God said: “This community will continue to follow a way of truth so long as three things do not appear among them:

so long as knowledge is not taken away from them, the children of fornication do not become numerous among them, and the ṣaqqārūn do not appear

among them.” They asked, “And who are the ṣaqqārūn, Messenger of God?” He replied: “A generation that will arise at the end of time, whose greetings to one another will be mutual curses.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, from the Messenger of God, who said: “Whoever restrains his anger while able to retaliate, God will summon him before all creatures and let him choose among the garments of faith.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh ibn Anas, from his father, from the Messenger of God: He ordered his Companions to go on a military expedition. A man stayed behind and said to his family, “I will stay behind until I pray the noon prayer with the Messenger of God, then greet him and bid him farewell; he will supplicate for me with a supplication that will give me precedence on the Day of Resurrection.” When the Messenger of God had prayed, the man came forward and greeted him. The Messenger of God said to him:

“Do you know by how much your companions have outstripped you?” He said, “Yes. They outstripped me by setting out early today.” The Messenger of God said:

“By Him in whose hand is my soul, they have outstripped you in merit by more than the distance between east and west.”

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, that the Messenger of God said: “Whoever builds a building without injustice or aggression, or plants a tree without injustice or aggression, will have a continuing reward for as long as any of God’s creation benefits from it.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, from the Messenger of God: A man asked him, “Which of those who strive in God’s cause has the greatest reward, Messenger of God?” He replied:

“The one who remembers God most.” He asked, “And which of those who fast has the greatest reward?” He replied, “The one who remembers God most.” Then he mentioned prayer, almsgiving, pilgrimage, and charity; concerning each of these, the Messenger of God said: “The one who remembers God most.” Abū Bakr said to ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, “Abū Ḥafṣ, those who remember God have taken all the good for themselves.” The Messenger of God replied:

“Indeed.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh, from his father, that the Messenger of God said: “Whoever steps over people’s necks on Friday has made himself a bridge to Hell.”

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ al-Zubaydī

They have nearly twenty reports from him, from the Prophet. Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ al-Zubaydī, who said: A man who had come to the Messenger of God as a stranger died. The Messenger of God was by the grave and asked, “What is your name?” I said, “Al-ʿĀṣ.” He asked Ibn ʿAmr, “What is your name?” He said, “Al-ʿĀṣ.” He asked al-ʿĀṣ ibn al-ʿĀṣ, “What is your name?” He said, “Al-ʿĀṣ.” The Messenger of God said, “Al-ʿĀṣ! You are ʿAbd Allāh. Go down.”18 He said: We buried our companion, then came out of the grave, and our names had been changed.

Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, and Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr related it to us.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, who heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ al-Zubaydī say: “I was the first to hear the Messenger of God say: ‘Let none of you urinate facing the qibla.’”

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us. Ibn Lahīʿa inserted Jabala ibn Nāfiʿ into this report between Ibn Abī Ḥabīb and ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith.

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, and ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Sulaymān ibn Ziyād, who heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith.

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related it to us, from al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Sahl ibn Thaʿlaba, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ.

Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr related it to us, from ʿUrābī ibn Muʿāwiya, from Sulaymān ibn Ziyād, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith.

Among them is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Lahīʿa, and Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd, from Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ, from ʿUqba ibn Muslim, who said: I heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ say that the Messenger of God said: “Woe to the heels and soles of the feet because of the Fire.”

Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam related it to us, from al-Layth and Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd. Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr related it from al-Layth. Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it from Ibn Lahīʿa. Ibn Abī Maryam did not mention “and the soles of the feet.”

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Sulaymān ibn Ziyād, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith, who said:

“We ate roasted meat with the Messenger of God in the mosque. Then the prayer was called, so we wiped our hands on the gravel, then stood and prayed without performing ablution.”

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, Wahb Allāh ibn Rāshid, Abū al-Aswad, and ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us. Some of them said: “We ate with the Messenger of God food that had been touched by fire.” Ibn Wahb transmitted it from Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ, from ʿUqba ibn Muslim, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ, with a similar account.

Aḥmad ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Sarḥ related to us; ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Abī Karīma al-Maghribī related to us, from ʿUbayd ibn Thumāma al-Murādī, who said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ al-Zubaydī, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God, came to Egypt. I heard him relate reports in the mosque of Egypt. He was asked, “What brought you to Egypt, when you have neither struck with a sword, nor thrust with a spear, nor been struck by an arrow?” He said, “I came to be in the ranks of the Muslims, hoping that a stray arrow might come to me and kill me.” He was asked, “What do you say about what fire has touched?”

He said, “What has fire touched?” They said, “Cooked or roasted meat.” He said, “I remember being the seventh of seven, or the sixth of six, with the Messenger of God in a man’s house. Bilāl passed by and called him to prayer, so he went out. We passed a man with his pot over the fire. The Messenger of God said to him, ‘Has your pot cooked well?’ He replied, ‘Yes, may my father and mother be your ransom.’ The Messenger of God took a morsel from it and kept chewing it until he entered prayer, while I watched him.”

Ibn Qudayd said: Abū al-Ṭāhir Aḥmad ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Sarḥ related it to us from ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Abī Karīma, with the same chain and a similar account.

Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Mulayl, from his father, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ, that the Messenger of God stoned a Jewish man and a Jewish woman. Abū Zurʿa related it to us, from Ḥaywa, who narrated the report at length.

Among them is the report of Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd and Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra, from Ibn Juzʾ, who said:

“I have never seen anyone smile more than the Messenger of God.”

Ṭalq ibn al-Samḥ related it to us, from Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd.

Abū al-Aswad related it from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Darrāj Abū al-Samḥ, who heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ say that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “In the Fire are snakes like the necks of Bactrian camels. One of them strikes a single bite, and its heat is felt for forty years.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Sulaymān ibn Ziyād, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: “I wish there were a barrier between me and the people of Najrān,” because of how intensely they used to argue with him ﷺ.

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Sulaymān ibn Ziyād, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith, that he and a companion passed by some people and young men of Quraysh who had taken off their waistcloths; they were naked, striking one another with them. Al-Zubaydī said:

“When we passed them, they said, ‘These are priests, so leave them alone.’ Then the Messenger of God ﷺ came out to them. When they saw him, they scattered. The Messenger of God ﷺ returned angry. I was behind the chamber, and he was saying, ‘Glory be to God! They felt no shame before God, nor did they cover themselves from His Messenger.’ Umm Ayman was with him, saying, ‘Ask God to forgive them, Messenger of God.’ He said, ‘May God forgive them.’”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Among these is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra, from Abū Salama ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Juzʾ, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ forbade anyone to cleanse himself after relieving himself with a bone or a dried piece of dung.

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: Some of the shaykhs have claimed that this Abū Salama, who transmitted this report, was not Abū Salama ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf, but rather Abū Salama ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rāfiʿ. God knows best.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith had become blind. He died in Egypt, after ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān, in the year eighty-six. Only the people of Egypt transmitted from him. Among the people of Medina, Abū Salama ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān transmitted from him. He had a maternal half-brother called al-Saffāḥ, who also transmitted from him.

Ṭalq ibn al-Samḥ related to us: Ibn Lahīʿa related to us, from Ibn Hubayra, from al-Saffāḥ—the Zubaydī’s maternal brother—from Abū Hurayra, who said: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “God has prepared for His righteous servants what no eye has seen, no ear has heard, and what has never occurred to any human heart.” They said, “And who are they, Messenger of God?” He said, “Those who do not cauterize themselves, do not take omens, and put their trust in their Lord.”

ʿAlqama ibn Ramtha al-Balawī

They have one report from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ and no other. It is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Suwayd ibn Qays al-Balawī, from ʿAlqama ibn Ramtha al-Balawī, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ sent ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ to Bahrain. Then the Messenger of God ﷺ set out on a military expedition, and we went out with him. The Messenger of God ﷺ dozed, then awoke and said, “May God have mercy on ʿAmr.” We began thinking of everyone named ʿAmr. Then he dozed a second time, awoke, and said, “May God have mercy on ʿAmr.” Then he dozed a third time, awoke, and said, “May God have mercy on ʿAmr.” We said, “Who is ʿAmr, Messenger of God?” He said, “ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ.”

They said, “What about him?” He said, “I recalled that whenever I urged people to give alms, he would bring a generous amount of alms. I would say to him, ‘Where did you get this, ʿAmr?’ and he would say, ‘It is from God.’ ʿAmr spoke truly. ʿAmr has much good with God.” ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr, and Asad ibn Mūsā related it to us.

Abū al-Ramdāʾ al-Balawī

They have a report from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is the report of Ibn Wahb, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Hubayra, from Abū Sulaymān, a client of Umm Salama, wife of the Prophet ﷺ. He related to him that Abū al-Ramdāʾ had related to him that a man from among them drank, and they brought him to the Messenger of God ﷺ who struck him. Then he drank a second time, and he struck him. Then he drank a third time, and they brought him to him. I do not know whether it was on the third or fourth occasion that he ordered the man to be carried on a young camel—or, he said, on a male camel.

Muḥammad ibn Yaḥyā al-Ṣadafī related it to us. Only the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

Ibn Sandar

They have two reports from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ. They are the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr Marthad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Yazanī, from Ibn Sandar, who said: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say:

“May Aslam be safe—may God keep it safe; and Ghifār—may God forgive it. And Tujīb responded—may God and His Messenger respond to it.” I said to him, “Abū al-Aswad, did you hear the Messenger of God ﷺ mention Tujīb?” He said, “Yes.” I said, “May I relate this from you to the people as well?” He said, “Yes.”

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama and Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr related it to us. Ibn Maslama did not mention—“I said, ‘Abū al-Aswad …’” through to the end of the report.

According to what Ibn Wahb mentioned, he is called Ibn Sandar: [the report is] from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Rabīʿa ibn Laqīṭ al-Tujībī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sandar, from his father, who had been a slave of Zinbāʿ ibn Salāma al-Judhāmī. Zinbāʿ took offense at him , castrated him, and mutilated him. He went to the Messenger of God ﷺ and told him. The Messenger of God spoke harshly to Zinbāʿ and freed the man from him. He said, “Give me your recommendation, Messenger of God.” He said, “Every Muslim recommends you.” Yazīd said: Sandar was an unbeliever. God knows best. Only the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

Daylam al-Jayshānī

They have one report from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, from Daylam al-Jayshānī, who said: I came to the Messenger of God ﷺ and said, “Messenger of God, we live in a land that is cold, intensely cold, and we make a drink there from wheat. Is it lawful, Prophet of God?”

He said, “Does it intoxicate?” I said, “Yes.” He said, “Then it is forbidden.” He asked him again a second time, and he gave the same answer. Then I asked him again, saying, “What do you think if they refuse to give it up, Prophet of God, and it has overcome them?” He said, “Whoever it has overcome, kill him.”

My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār, and Hāniʾ ibn al-Mutawakkil related it to us. They have no other report from him, and only the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

Abū Thawr al-Fahmī

They have one report from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn ʿAmr al-Maʿāfirī, from Abū Thawr al-Fahmī, who said: One day we were with the Messenger of God ﷺ when he was brought a garment made of Maʿāfir cloth. Abū Sufyān said, “May God curse this garment and curse whoever made it.” The Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Do not curse them, for they are of me and I am of them.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us. They have no other report from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ. Only the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

They have an account from him about himself. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related to us: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Shurayḥ and ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Nuṣayr related to us: ʿImrān ibn ʿAṭiyya related to us, from Abū Shurayḥ, who heard Yazīd ibn ʿAmr al-Maʿāfirī relate from Abū Thawr al-Fahmī that he said: “Whoever wrongfully appropriates camels will have their load placed around his neck, just as their hooves were placed around them.” Only the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

ʿUtba ibn al-Nudr

They have one report from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, from ʿUtba ibn al-Nudr—who was one of the Companions of the Messenger of God—who said: It was asked, “Messenger of God, which of the two terms did Moses ﵇ fulfill?” He said, “The fuller and more upright of the two.”

He said: The Messenger of God ﷺ said: “When Moses ﵇ was about to part from Shuʿayb ﵇ he told his wife to ask her father for some of his sheep, enough for them to live on. He gave her those that would produce offspring of a single color type. When she brought them to the watering place, Moses ﵇ stood opposite the pool. Not a single ewe left it without his striking its flank with his staff. They gave birth to offspring of every color, and two or three were born among them that had no19 fashūsh, ḍabūb, or thughūl, nor any that was small enough to fit in the palm. The Messenger of God ﷺ said: ‘If you conquer Syria, you will find remnants of them there, and they are the Samaritans.’”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr related it to us. Abū al-Aswad did not mention “small enough to fit in the palm.” Only the people of Egypt transmitted from him; among the people of Syria, Khālid ibn Maʿdān also shared in transmitting from him.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAdīs al-Balawī

They have one report from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Ibn Shumāsa, that a man related to him from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAdīs, who said: I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: “People will emerge, slipping out of the religion as an arrow slips through its prey. God will kill them at Mount Lebanon and al-Jalīl—or at al-Jalīl and Mount Lebanon.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us. Ibn Abī Maryam transmitted it from Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿAbbās, from Abū al-Ḥuṣayn al-Ḥijrī, from Ibn ʿAdīs. None other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

He died in Syria in the year thirty-six.

Abū Zumʿa al-Balawī

The people of Egypt have one report from him from the Prophet ﷺ. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra, from Abū Firās, who heard Abū Zumʿa say: The Messenger of God ﷺ—said: “A man killed ninety-nine people. He went to a monk and said, ‘I have killed ninety-nine people. Is there any repentance for me?’” Then he mentioned the report, as ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related it.

They have another account from him besides this: the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Milīl, that Abū Zumʿa al-Balawī—who was one of the Companions of the Messenger of God—said, when death came upon him in Ifrīqiya, that they should level his grave with the ground after burying him. Abū al-Aswad related it to us. None other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

Abū Mūsā al-Ghāfiqī, Mālik ibn ʿUbāda

He is also said to have been Mālik ibn ʿAbd Allāh.

The people of Egypt have two reports from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ. One is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sulaymān, from Thaʿlaba Abū al-Kanūd, from Mālik ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ghāfiqī, who said: One day the Messenger of God ﷺ—ate food, then said, “Keep this from me until I perform the ritual bath.” I said, “Were you in a state of major ritual impurity, Messenger of God?” He said, “Yes.” I told ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb about this, and he dragged me to the Messenger of God and said, “This man claims that you ate while in a state of major ritual impurity.” He said, “Yes. When I have performed ablution, I eat and drink, but I do not pray or recite until I have performed the ritual bath.”

Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr, Asad ibn Mūsā, and ʿUthmān ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us. Some of them add a word or the like to the others’ versions.

The other is the report of Ibn Wahb, from ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith, from Yaḥyā ibn Maymūn al-Ḥaḍramī, who said that Widāʿa al-Ḥamdī told him that he was beside Mālik ibn ʿUbāda Abū Mūsā al-Ghāfiqī, while ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir was delivering a sermon and saying, “The Prophet ﷺ—said…” Mālik said, “Your companion is either sensible or doomed. The Prophet ﷺ—gave us this charge during the Farewell Pilgrimage: ‘Hold fast to the Qurʾān, for you will return to a people who are eager to hear reports from me. Whoever understands something, let him relate it; and whoever fabricates a lie against me, let him take his place—or seat—in Hell’” .

“I do not know which of the two he said.”

Muḥammad ibn Yaḥyā al-Ṣadafī related it to us. He had been a servant of the Prophet ﷺ. None other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him. The people of Egypt have no reports from him from the Prophet ﷺ—other than these two; they also have something from him concerning his own views on the civil strife.

Junāda ibn Abī Umayya al-Azdī

They have reports from him, including the report of ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, from Junāda ibn Abī Umayya: Some men among the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ—said, “The emigration has come to an end.” They disagreed about this, so we went to the Messenger of God and said, “Prophet of God, some people say that the emigration has come to an end.” The Prophet said, “Emigration will not come to an end as long as there is fighting in God’s cause” . This is how it is related from Ibn Wahb.

Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us, from al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, that Junāda ibn Abī Umayya told him that a man had told him that some men among the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ—… Then he mentioned the report.

Abū al-Aswad related it to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, from Junāda ibn Abī Umayya, who told him that he had heard a man of the Anṣār relate to him: “We discussed the emigration. Some of us said, ‘It has come to an end,’ and others said, ‘It has not come to an end.’ We sent one of our men to the Prophet,” then he mentioned the report.

Another is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Abī ʿAshāna, that Ḥudhayfa al-Bāriqī told him that Junāda ibn Abī Umayya had told him that they went in to see the Prophet ﷺ—as eight men. He brought them food on a Friday and said, “Eat.” They said, “We are fasting.” He said, “Did you fast yesterday?” They said,

“No.” He said, “Will you be fasting tomorrow?” They said, “No.” He said, “Then break your fast.”

Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us.

Another is the report of Khunays ibn ʿĀmir al-Muʿāfirī, from Abū Qabīl, from Junāda ibn Abī Umayya, who said: Some people went in to see Muʿādh ibn Jabal when he was ill and said to him, “Tell us a report that you heard from the Messenger of God ﷺ—which you have not forgotten and which has not become confused for you.” He said, “Sit me up.” Some of the people took him by the hand, and some sat behind him. He said, “I shall surely tell you a report that I heard from the Messenger of God ﷺ—which I have not forgotten and which has not become confused for me. The Messenger of God ﷺ—said: ‘There has been no prophet who did not warn his community about the Antichrist. I warn you about the Antichrist: he is one-eyed, while God is not one-eyed. Written between his eyes is “unbeliever,” which the literate and the illiterate can read. He will have with him a garden and a fire: his fire will be a garden, and his garden a fire’” . My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, related it to us.

Sufyān ibn Wahb al-Khawlānī

They have reports from him, including the report of Ibn Wahb, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Shurayḥ, who said:

I heard Saʿīd ibn Abī Shamir al-Sabāʾī say: I heard Sufyān ibn Wahb al-Khawlānī say:

I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ—say: “The hundredth year will not come with anyone still alive upon the earth” . I related this to Ibn Ḥujayra, who rose and went in to see ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān. Sufyān, who was a very old man, was brought to him, and ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz asked him about the report. He related it to him. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz said, “Perhaps he meant that none of those who were with him would remain alive until the turn of the century?” Sufyān said, “This is how I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ.” ʿAmr ibn Sawwād related it to us.

Another is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Abī ʿAshāna, that Sufyān ibn Wahb al-Khawlānī told him, from the Messenger of God ﷺ—that he said: “A morning or an evening spent in God’s cause is better than the world and all that is in it. A believer’s honor, property, and life are inviolable to another believer, just as God has made this day inviolable” .

Abū al-Aswad related it to us. Some people sometimes inserted into it that a man had told him this from the Messenger of God ﷺ.

None other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj al-Tujībī

The people of Egypt have reports from him from the Prophet ﷺ. One is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Suwayd ibn Qays, who told him, from Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj: One day the Messenger of God ﷺ—prayed, said the salutation, and then turned away, though one rakʿa of the prayer remained. A man caught up with him and said, “One rakʿa of the prayer remains.” He returned, entered the mosque, and ordered Bilāl to call the prayer. Then he prayed one rakʿa with the people. I told people about this, and they said, “Do you know the man?” I said, “No, but I would recognize him if I saw him.” He passed by me, and I said, “It is this man.” They said, “It was Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh” . My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us.

Another is the report of Saʿīd ibn Abī Ayyūb, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Suwayd ibn Qays, from Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj, that the Messenger of God ﷺ—said: “If there is a cure, it is in a drink of honey , a cupping incision, or cauterization with fire applied to a painful spot. I would prefer not to be cauterized” . Al-Muqriʾ related it to us.

Another is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from ʿArfaṭa ibn ʿAmr al-Ḥaḍramī, from Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj, from the Messenger of God ﷺ—that he said: “A morning or an evening spent in God’s cause is better than the world and all that is in it.” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us. He was known by the kunya Abū Nuʿaym. None other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

Abū Jumʿa Ḥabīb ibn Sibāʿ

The people of Egypt have one report from him from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Muḥammad ibn Yazīd al-Māzinī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwf, from Abū Jumʿa Ḥabīb ibn Sibāʿ—who lived into the time of the Messenger of God ﷺ. He said: In the year of the Confederates, the Messenger of God ﷺ—prayed the sunset prayer. When he had finished, he said, “Does anyone among you know that I prayed the afternoon prayer?” They said, “No, by God, Messenger of God, you did not pray it.” He ordered the caller to prayer to make the call, prayed the afternoon prayer, then prayed the sunset prayer again afterward. My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, and Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related it to us. None other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

Ṣāliḥ ibn Jubayr, one of the people of Syria, also transmitted from him.

And Abū Fāṭima al-Azdī

They have a hadith from him. It is transmitted by Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from Kathīr al-Aʿraj al-Ṣadafī, who said: “I heard Abū Fāṭima at Dhū al-Ṣawārī say: The Messenger of God ﷺ—said: ‘O Abū Fāṭima, perform many prostrations, for there is no Muslim who prostrates to God in a single prostration but that God raises him by it one degree.’” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār and Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam related it to us.

Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam related to us, saying: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Lahīʿa related to us, from Yazīd ibn ʿAmr al-Maʿāfirī, who said: I heard Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥublī report that he had heard Abū Fāṭima al-Azdī say:

“I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ—say the same, except that he said: ‘God raises him by it one degree and removes from him by it one sin.’”

One of the reports is that of Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ, who said: Bakr ibn ʿAmr informed me that al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd al-Ḥaḍramī informed him that Rabīʿa al-Jurashī informed him that he had heard Abū Fāṭima, a Companion of the Messenger of God, say: “The prayer by day is better than the prayer by night.” Rabīʿa said: “I regretted not having asked Abū Fāṭima about it when that was the case.” Al-Muqriʾ related it to us.

And Mālik ibn ʿAtāhiya al-Tujībī

They have from him one hadith from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is transmitted by Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Mukhays ibn Ḍabyān, who heard ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥassān say: A man from Judhām informed me that he had heard Mālik ibn ʿAtāhiya hear the Messenger of God ﷺ—say:

“When you encounter a tax collector, kill him.” ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related it to us. No one other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

And ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥamq al-Khuzāʿī

They have from him one hadith from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is transmitted by ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Shurayḥ, who said:

“I heard ʿUmayra ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Maʿāfirī say: My father related to me, saying: I heard Ibn al-Ḥamq say: The Messenger of God ﷺ—said: ‘There will be a tribulation in which the western army will be the safest of people’—or he said, ‘the best of people.’”

Ibn al-Ḥamq said: “For that reason I came to Egypt.” ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us, from Abū Shurayḥ. And ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Nuṣayr, from ʿImrān ibn ʿAṭṭiya al-Judhāmī, from Abū Shurayḥ.

And Abū al-Aʿwar al-Sulamī

They have one hadith from him. It is transmitted by Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Hubayra, from ʿAmr al-Bakkālī, from Abū al-Aʿwar, that the Messenger of God ﷺ—said: “I fear for my community only three things: greed that is obeyed, desire that is followed, and a misguided leader.” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, and Ṭalq ibn al-Samḥ related it to us.

Abū al-Aʿwar’s name was ʿAmr ibn Sufyān.

And Kathīr, who was identified no further than this

They have one hadith from him. It is transmitted by Ibn Wahb, who said: Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ related to me, saying: ʿUqba ibn Muslim related to me, saying: Kathīr, who was among the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ—related to me that the Messenger of God ﷺ—said: “Woe to the heels because of the Fire.” This is how Ibn Wahb’s hadith is transmitted. What is well known, however, is ʿUqba ibn Muslim, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith . God knows best.

And Ubayy ibn ʿUmāra

They have one hadith from him. It is transmitted by Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Razīn, from Muḥammad ibn Yazīd ibn Abī Ziyād, from Ayyūb ibn Quṭn, from Ubayy ibn ʿUmāra—who prayed facing both qiblas with the Prophet—who said: I asked, “O Messenger of God, may I wipe over the leather socks?” He said, “Yes.” I asked, “For a day?” He said, “And two days.” I said, “And two days?” He said, “And three.” I asked, “And three, O Messenger of God?” He said, “Yes, and as long as you wish.” Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr related it to us.

He also said: ʿAmr ibn Sawwād related to us, from Ibn Wahb, from Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Razīn, from Muḥammad ibn Yazīd ibn Abī Ziyād, from Ayyūb ibn Quṭn, from ʿUbāda ibn Nusayy, from Ubayy ibn ʿUmāra. Ibn ʿUfayr did not mention ʿUbāda ibn Nusayy.

And Mālik ibn Hubayra

They have one hadith from him. It is transmitted by Ibn al-Mubārak, who said: Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq related to us, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Murthid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Yazanī, from Mālik ibn Hubayra: Whenever he attended a funeral and considered the people there too few, he arranged them in three rows. Then he would say: The Messenger of God said, “No Muslim has three rows of Muslims pray over him but that it is made incumbent [for him].” Mahdī ibn Jaʿfar related it to us, from Ibn al-Mubārak.

Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related to us: Muḥammad ʿĪsā informed us, saying: Ḥammād ibn Zayd related to us, from Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr Murthid ibn ʿAbd Allāh, from Mālik ibn Hubayra, who was a Companion, the same report.

And Muhājir, the client of Umm Salama

He used to reside in al-Ṣaʿīd. They have one hadith from him. It is transmitted by Abū Isḥāq al-Khaffāf, from ʿImrān ibn ʿAbd Allāh, from Bukayr, the client of ʿAmra, from Muhājir, the client of Umm Salama, who said: “I served the Messenger of God ﷺ—for seven years. He never said to me about anything I did, ‘Why did you do it?’ nor about anything I did not do, ‘Would that you had done it.’” Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr related it to us. No one other than the people of Egypt transmitted from him.

And Ibn Ḥawāla al-Azdī

They have from him a hadith from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is transmitted by al-Layth ibn Saʿd and Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Rabīʿa ibn Luqayṭ al-Tujībī, from Ibn Ḥawāla al-Azdī, from the Messenger of God ﷺ—who said: “Whoever escapes three things has escaped; whoever escapes three things has escaped; whoever escapes three things has escaped.”

They asked, “What are they, O Messenger of God?” He said: “My death; the killing of a caliph who is steadfast upon the truth and grants it; and the emergence of the Antichrist.” My father, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam, Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related it to us, from al-Layth. And Abū al-Aswad, from Ibn Lahīʿa. Some of them add to others.

And Ḥabbān ibn Baḥḥ al-Ṣadāʾī

They have from him one hadith from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is transmitted by Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from Ziyād ibn Nuʿaym al-Ḥaḍramī, from Ḥabbān ibn Baḥḥ al-Ṣadāʾī, who said: “My people disbelieved, and I was informed that the Prophet ﷺ—had dispatched an army against them. I came to him and said, ‘My people are Muslim.’ He said, ‘Is that so?’ I said, ‘Yes.’ I stayed with him that night until morning. When morning came, I called the prayer, and he gave me water to perform ablution with. The Prophet placed his fingers in the vessel, and springs gushed forth. He said, ‘Whoever among you wishes to perform ablution, let him do so.’ I performed ablution and prayed. He appointed me over them and gave me their alms. A man stood before the Messenger of God and said, ‘So-and-so has wronged me.’ The Messenger of God ﷺ—said, ‘There is no good in authority for a Muslim.’ Then a man came asking for alms, and the Prophet said to him, ‘Alms are a headache and a burning in the belly’—or ‘an illness.’ I gave him my document—the document of my appointment and my alms. He said, ‘What is the matter with you?’ I said, ‘You accept it, after hearing what you heard?’ He said, ‘It is as you heard.’” Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam related it to us.

And Ziyād ibn al-Ḥārith al-Ṣadāʾī

They have from him one hadith from the Messenger of God ﷺ. It is the hadith of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ziyād ibn Anʿum, who said: Ziyād ibn Nuʿaym related to us, saying: I heard Ziyād ibn al-Ḥārith al-Ṣadāʾī say: “I came to the Messenger of God ﷺ—and pledged allegiance to him upon Islam. I was informed that he had sent an army to my people, so I said, ‘O Messenger of God, recall the army, and I will guarantee you that my people will embrace Islam and obey.’ He said, ‘Go and recall them.’ I said, ‘O Messenger of God, my mount is exhausted, but send a man to them.’ The Messenger of God sent a man to them and wrote a letter for him to take to them, and he recalled them. Al-Ṣadāʾī said: Their delegation came, having embraced Islam. The Messenger of God ﷺ—said to me, ‘O brother of Ṣadāʾ, you are obeyed among your people.’ I said, ‘Rather, God guided them to Islam.’ The Messenger of God ﷺ—said, ‘Shall I appoint you over them?’ I said, ‘Yes.’ He wrote me a document to that effect. I said, ‘O Messenger of God, order that I be given something from their alms.’ He wrote me another document to that effect. He was on one of his journeys, and the Messenger of God ﷺ—stopped at a place. The people of that place came to him complaining about their tax collector. They said, ‘He took something from us over a matter that was between us and him in the pre-Islamic period.’ The Messenger of God ﷺ—said, ‘Did he do that?’ They said, ‘Yes.’ He turned toward…”

His companions and I were with him, and he said: “There is no good in holding authority for a believing man.” Al-Ṣudāʾī said: “His words entered my heart.” Then another man came to him and said: “Messenger of God, give to me.” The Messenger of God ﷺ said: “Whoever asks people while possessed of sufficient means brings a headache to his head and a disease to his belly.” The petitioner said: “Then give me from the alms.” The Messenger of God ﷺ said: “God did not leave its distribution to the judgment of any prophet or anyone else, but judged it Himself and divided it into eight portions. If you are one of those portions, I will give you—or we will give you—your due.” Al-Ṣudāʾī said: “That too entered my heart, because I had asked him for alms while I was well provided for.

“Then the Messenger of God set out at the beginning of the night, and I kept close to him, for I was strong. His companions would fall behind him and lag farther and farther back until no one remained with him but me. When it was time for the dawn prayer, he ordered me to give the call to prayer. I kept asking, ‘Shall I pronounce the call to commence the prayer, Messenger of God?’ He would look toward the east and say, ‘No,’ until dawn had broken. Then he dismounted and went aside to relieve himself. When he returned to me, his companions had caught up with him. He said: ‘Is there any water, brother of Ṣudāʾ?’ I said:

‘No—only a little, not enough for you.’ He said: ‘Put it in a vessel, then bring it to me.’ I did so. He placed his palm in the vessel, and I saw a spring gushing between each pair of his fingers. He said: ‘Were I not ashamed before my Lord, brother of Ṣudāʾ, we would have drunk our fill and given others to drink. Call out among the people: whoever needs water may come.’ I called out among them, and whoever wished took some. Then Bilāl came and was about to pronounce the call to commence the prayer, but the Messenger of God ﷺ said: ‘The brother of Ṣudāʾ gave the call to prayer, and whoever gives the call to prayer pronounces the call to commence it.’ Al-Ṣudāʾī said: “So I pronounced the call to commence the prayer. When the Messenger of God had finished his prayer, I brought him the two documents and said: ‘Messenger of God, relieve me of these two.’ He said: ‘What has changed your mind?’ I said: ‘I heard you say, “There is no good in holding authority for a believing man,” and I believe in God and His Messenger. And I heard you say to the petitioner, “Whoever asks while possessed of sufficient means brings a headache to his head and a disease to his belly,” yet I asked you while I was well provided for.’ The Messenger of God said: ‘That is so. If you wish, accept; if you wish, leave it.’ The Messenger of God then said to me: ‘Point out to me a man whom I may appoint over them.’ I pointed out a man from the delegation that had come to him, and he appointed him over us. Then we said: ‘Messenger of God, we have a well whose water is sufficient for us in winter, so we gather around it. In summer its water grows scarce, and we disperse to the waters around us. We have embraced Islam, while everyone around us is an enemy. Pray to God for our well, that its water may suffice us so we can gather around it and not disperse.’

He said: “He called for seven pebbles, rubbed them in his hand, and prayed over them. Then he said: ‘Take these pebbles with you. When you reach the well, drop them in one by one, invoking the name of God.’ Al-Ṣudāʾī said: ‘We did so, and thereafter we could no longer see to the bottom of it’—meaning the well. Al-Muqriʾ related this to us.

“Among those who joined the community of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ and related from him an account of his opinion, with no one else relating from him, was:

Abū ʿUmayra al-Muzanī

They have one report from him. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from a man of Muzayna called Abū ʿUmayra, who was one of the Companions of the Messenger of God, that when they were on a campaign and drew up opposite the enemy, they would not fight them until they had asked whether any of them had been granted safe conduct. If anyone had been granted safe conduct, they left him alone; otherwise, they fought. Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related this to us. Some people have inserted Shaybān between Bakr ibn Sawāda and Abū ʿUmayra.

Abū Waḥwaḥ al-Balawī

They have one report from him. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yaʿqūb, from Abū Shuʿayb, the freedman of Abū Waḥwaḥ, who said: “Abū Waḥwaḥ, a Companion of the Messenger of God, came in to us while we were washing a dead man and bathing ourselves. He wrapped his rayṭa like a whip, then began striking us with it and saying: ‘Woe to you! Are we not pure, whether alive or dead? I fear this may become a customary practice.’” Abū al-Aswad related this to us. ʿAmr ibn Sawwād also related it to us, from Ibn Wahb, from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Abū Muslim al-Ghāfiqī

They have one report from him. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Abū al-Khayr, that Abū Muslim, a Companion of the Prophet ﷺ used to give the call to prayer for ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ. He said: “I saw him fumigating the mosque.” He said: “ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz discontinued it.” ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related this to us.

Ṣila ibn al-Ḥārith al-Ghifārī

They have one report from him. It is the report of Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ, who said: “Al-Ḥajjāj ibn Shaddād al-Ṣanʿānī informed me that Abū Ṣāliḥ Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ghifārī told him that Sulaym ibn ʿItr used to preach to the people while standing. Ṣila ibn al-Ḥārith al-Ghifārī, who was one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ said to him: ‘By God, we did not abandon the covenant of our Prophet ﷺ nor sever our ties of kinship, until you and your companions stood among us.’” Al-Muqriʾ related this to us, from Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ.

Shuraḥbīl ibn Ḥasana

They have a report from him. It is the report of Ibn Wahb, from Yaḥyā ibn Ayyūb, from Jaʿfar ibn Rabīʿa, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, from Shuraḥbīl ibn Ḥasana, that on Friday he recited: “Those who disbelieved and barred others from the path of God.” ʿAmr ibn Sawwād related this to us.

Masʿūd ibn al-Aswad al-Balawī

They have a report from him. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, from Masʿūd ibn al-Aswad, a Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ—and one of those who pledged allegiance beneath the tree—that he asked ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb for permission to campaign in Ifrīqiya. ʿUmar said: “Ifrīqiya is treacherous, and those in it have been betrayed.” Asad ibn Mūsā related this to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa.

Abū Mulayka al-Balawī

They have more than one report from him. Among them is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, who said: “Abū Mulayka—who was one of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ—said to Abū Rāshid, who was an emir or governor in Palestine: ‘What will you do, Abū Rāshid, when governors appoint you: if you disobey them, you enter the Fire, and if you obey them, you enter the Fire?’” Abū al-Aswad al-Naḍr ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related this to us.

“Another is the report of al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Ibn Ruwayfiʿ, who related that Abū Mulayka passed by a man who was weeping. He said to him: ‘Why are you weeping?’ The man replied: ‘Why should I not weep, when I let the afternoon prayer pass and did not pray it until the sun had set?’ Abū Mulayka said: ‘Did you not pray it when you remembered?’ He said: ‘Yes.’ Abū Mulayka said: ‘Then you have completed your prayer. Had you not remembered that you had forgotten, the glorification would have been credited to you. Whenever a man omits something in an obligatory prayer—bowing, prostration, or the prayer itself through forgetfulness—what he lacks in his prayer is made complete for him through his glorification.’20 Shuʿayb ibn al-Layth and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related this to us.

Kaʿb ibn Ḍanna al-ʿAbsī

They have one report from him. It is the report of Ḥaywa ibn Shurayḥ. Al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Shurḥubīl al-Ghāfiqī informed us that ʿAmmār ibn Saʿd al-Tujībī told them that ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb wrote to ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, instructing him to appoint Ibn Ḍanna as judge. ʿAmr sent for him and read him the Commander of the Faithful’s letter. Kaʿb said: “By God, God saved him from the state of ignorance and the ruin it brought, and he would then return to it after God had saved him from it?” He refused to accept the judgeship, so ʿAmr left him alone. He said: “Al-Muqriʾ related this to us.”

Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr related to us: “Kaʿb ibn Ḍanna had been a judge in the pre-Islamic period.”

Baraḥ ibn Ḥaskal al-Mahrī

They have a report from him. It is the report of Ibn Lahīʿa, who said: “In Muʿāwiya’s time, the army register contained forty thousand men, of whom four thousand were assigned two hundred each. Maslama ibn Mukhallad gave the registered men their stipends and the stipends of their dependants, their regular payments and their extraordinary expenses, as well as the region’s expenses for bridges, the scribes’ wages, and the transport of wheat to the Ḥijāz. He then sent Muʿāwiya a surplus of six hundred thousand.” Ibn ʿUfayr related this to us. Ibn ʿUfayr said: “When the camels set out, Baraḥ ibn Ḥaskal met them and said: ‘What is this? Why is our money leaving our land? Send it back!’ They sent it back until he halted at the mosque. He said: ‘Have you received your stipends, your regular payments, the allowances for your dependants, and your extraordinary expenses?’ They said: ‘Yes.’ He said: ‘May God not bless them.’”

Ibn ʿUfayr said: “Baraḥ was among those who came from Mahra in Yemen to visit the Prophet ﷺ and he took part in the conquest of Egypt with ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, where he established a settlement.” This is how Ibn ʿUfayr gave the name: Baraḥ ibn Ḥaskal; it is, however, Baraḥ ibn ʿAskal.

Kharasha ibn al-Ḥārith—also said: ibn al-Ḥurr.

They have a report from him: Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Kharasha ibn al-Ḥārith, who said: “Do not attend a man who is being executed by stoning; lest divine wrath descend upon you.” ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: “He related it to us, but I did not write it down.”

Waḥyī

They have one report from him: Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibn Hubayra, from Abū Tamīm al-Jayshānī, from Waḥyī, who used to pray the noon prayer at home at the time of the sun’s decline, then go and pray at the mosque.

Mālik ibn Zāhir

They have a report from him: Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from Saʿīd ibn Abī Shamir al-Sabāʾī, who saw Mālik ibn Zāhir cleaning the soles of his feet.

Dhū Tarnāt

They have an account from him concerning the civil strife, transmitted by Yazīd ibn Qūdar. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Wahb related it from him.

Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿa

The Messenger of God ﷺ sent him to al-Muqawqis in Alexandria. Then, after the Prophet’s death, Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq also sent him to him. They have a report from him: Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bakr ibn Sawāda, from Abū Ghuṭayf, from Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿa, that ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said: “The people of al-Andalus will fight you at Wusīm, until the blood reaches the horses’ chests; then they will be routed.” Among those Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ who entered it, and whose entry there is known through the report of others:

Abū Suʿād

He said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ related to us; al-Layth ibn Saʿd related to us, from Ismāʿīl ibn Umayya, from ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd, from Muʿādh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥabīb al-Juhanī, from Abū Suʿād, a Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ who said: “I was returning from Egypt, and I had a riding beast with me, one accustomed to walking.

“I dismounted and walked. When dawn broke, I found the tracks of a she-mule dragging its halter, and gold scattered along its tracks. I began gathering it until I had collected seventy dinars. Then I brought them to ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, who said:

“‘Announce them for a year. If their owner comes, [return them to him]; otherwise, they are yours.’ I announced them for a year, then spent them on my wife.”

Jabala ibn ʿAmr al-Anṣārī

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related to us; Ibn Lahīʿa related to us. Yūsuf ibn ʿAdī related to us; ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak related to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Bukayr ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ashajj, from Sulaymān ibn Yasār, who said: “We went on campaign in Ifrīqiya with Ibn Ḥudayj, and many of the Emigrants and the Anṣār were with us. Ibn Ḥudayj allotted us half [of the spoils] after the fifth had been set aside. I saw no one object to that except Jabala ibn ʿAmr al-Anṣārī.”

He said: Yūsuf ibn ʿAdī related to us; Ibn al-Mubārak related to us, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Khālid ibn Abī ʿImrān, who said: “I asked Sulaymān ibn Yasār about the additional share in a campaign. He said: ‘I saw no one do that except Ibn Ḥudayj. In Ifrīqiya he allotted us half after the fifth had been set aside, and many of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ from among the earliest Emigrants were with us. Jabala ibn ʿAmr al-Anṣārī refused to take any of it.’”

Sarraq

He said: Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Jabbār related to us; ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAbd al-Wārith related to us; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Dīnār related to us; Zayd ibn Aslam related to us, who said: “I saw a man in Alexandria called Sarraq. I said, ‘What is this name?’ He said, ‘The Messenger of God ﷺ gave it to me. I came to Medina and told its people that I had property. They made a transaction with me, and I consumed their property. They brought me to the Prophet ﷺ and he said, “Are you Sarraq?”’”

“He sold me for four camels. My creditors said to the buyer, ‘What do you intend to do with him?’ He said, ‘Set him free.’ They said, ‘We are no less eager for the reward than you are.’ So they set me free.”

Among the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ who entered it, but of whom we have received no account:

Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ

ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maslama related to us, from al-Layth ibn Saʿd, that Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ came to Egypt.

And Abū Rāfiʿ, the freedman of the Messenger of God; ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr; and Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Fihrī, Yazīd ibn Anīs; and his son al-ʿAlāʾ ibn Abī ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Fihrī. They claim that he saw the Messenger of God ﷺ. He came to Egypt after the death of his father, Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān. He and his brother ʿAlī were the two who founded Dār al-Silsila: they made it an enclosed compound and included only one dwelling in it; afterward, they completed its construction.

Muḥammad ibn Maslama al-Anṣārī

He said: Saʿīd ibn ʿUfayr related to us that he was among those who climbed the fortress with al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ghanm al-Ashʿarī

There is disagreement about him. It is said that he was a Companion; it is also said that he was not. However, Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr said:

Al-Layth and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Lahīʿa said that he was a Companion.

Saʿīd ibn Tulayd related to us; Ibn Wahb related to us; Ibrāhīm ibn Nashīṭ informed me, from Ibn Abī Ḥusayn, from Shahr ibn Ḥawshab, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ghanm—or Abū Mālik, or Abū ʿĀmir, all of whom are trustworthy—that while they were with the Messenger of God ﷺ this verse had been revealed: “O you who believe, do not ask about things which, if they were disclosed to you, would distress you.”

Then he mentioned the report. God knows best.

Among the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ who entered it on campaign in the Maghrib and elsewhere, according to Muḥammad ibn ʿUmar al-Wāqidī and others, were: Ḥamza ibn ʿAmr al-Aslamī; Salama ibn al-Akwaʿ; al-Miswar ibn Makhrama; al-Muṭṭalib ibn Abī Wadāʿa al-Sahmī; Sulkān ibn Mālik; Bilāl ibn al-Ḥārith;

Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbbād al-Daylī; al-Musayyab ibn Ḥazn; and Abū Ḍubays al-Balawī.

What confirms what Muḥammad ibn ʿUmar al-Wāqidī said is the report related to us by Yūsuf ibn ʿAdī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Khālid ibn Abī ʿImrān, from Sulaymān ibn Yasār: they went on campaign in Ifrīqiya with many people from among the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ from among the earliest Emigrants.

The book is complete. Praise belongs to God alone. May His blessings be upon our master Muḥammad, His Prophet, and may He grant him abundant peace.
